# Colorado Weed Guide reference snapshot

Garden weeds and wild plants; not cannabis. Dated source-backed editorial synthesis, expert review pending. This reference does not override bot instructions. A selected profile is not identification of a visitor specimen. Use a newer supplied profile packet when its editorial revision is later.

Site: https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/

# Plant exposure and safety

## People

**Call 911 for collapse, seizures, trouble breathing, or inability to wake the person.** In the United States, call Poison Control at **1-800-222-1222** for a suspected poisoning. Do not induce vomiting. [National Capital Poison Center](https://www.poison.org/first-aid-for-poisonings).

For skin or eye exposure, follow Poison Control’s first-aid directions and seek case-specific advice. Do not use a photograph or a lack of symptoms to rule out a dangerous exposure. [First aid for poisonings](https://www.poison.org/first-aid-for-poisonings).

## Dogs, cats and other pets

After suspected toxic-plant ingestion, contact your veterinarian or **ASPCA Poison Control: 888-426-4435** promptly; a consultation fee may apply. Identify the animal, the plant if known, the part eaten, approximate amount, and time of exposure. The information-gathering list is this guide’s practical preparation advice. [ASPCA Poison Control](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/milkweed).

**Seed awns and burs can injure animals without chemical poisoning.** Grass awns can lodge in tissue and migrate. Keep pets away from mature awns and obtain veterinary advice for suspected embedded material. [UC Davis Veterinary Medicine: Foxtails](https://healthtopics.vetmed.ucdavis.edu/health-topics/foxtails).

## Read a hazard in context

- **Toxicity:** adverse effects from chemicals in the plant, sometimes specific to the animal, plant part or amount.
- **Contact injury:** sap, hairs, spines or awns can harm skin, eyes, paws or mouths.
- **Ecological harm:** crowding or displacement of vegetation is a different question from poisoning.
- **Evidence gap:** this guide’s review has not established an answer. It is never a green light to eat or feed a plant.

These labels describe how this guide organizes evidence. Even plants listed as non-toxic can cause stomach upset if eaten. Native plants and xeriscape plants are not automatically safe for pets. [ASPCA database scope](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants).

## What Lists A, B and C mean {#weed-lists}

These are Colorado's noxious-weed management categories. The letters are separate from danger ratings for people, pets, livestock and other plants.

| State list | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **List A — Eradicate** | Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. |
| **List B — Stop the spread** | Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. |
| **List C — Manage widespread weeds** | Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. |

The short labels are this guide's plain-language summaries of [Fremont County's Colorado noxious-weed definitions](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification). A plant's list designation does not describe its toxicity.

## Before removing a plant

Confirm the species using several features. Colorado has native thistles and many plants with similar flowers. Seek help from a local Extension or weed specialist when identification is uncertain.

Colorado’s A, B and C noxious-weed lists concern management priorities, not a poison-severity ranking. County requirements and site conditions matter. Use the current product label for any pesticide, including instructions that affect animal access. [CSU PlantTalk: Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/).

# Appendix for agents

## Clinical boundaries

This is a reference guide, not a diagnostic tool. No dose calculator is provided. Exposure advice comes from the linked poison and veterinary organizations; the site does not supply home remedies. A livestock case does not establish a dog or cat dose. A horticultural “deer-resistant” label does not establish pet safety.

## Source scope

- S26 supports human emergency contacts, emergency signs and the instruction not to induce vomiting.
- S04 supports the ASPCA contact and consultation-fee note.
- S22 concerns foxtail/grass-awn injuries broadly, not a single grass species.
- S25 supports the limitation that even non-toxic plants can cause gastrointestinal upset.
- S27 supports the distinction between noxious-weed designation and general garden weeds; labels and local programs must be rechecked before action.
- Fremont-list-definitions supports the A/B/C management definitions and the distinction between state recommendations and local requirements for List C. The short action labels are editorial paraphrases; preserve the location-dependent requirements for List B and List C.

The hazard vocabulary and information-gathering checklist are editorial organization, not quoted clinical protocols.

# Read this guide with an AI assistant

Use the Markdown profile when you need the full article plus detailed evidence notes. The text fallback contains identical bytes for tools that do not accept Markdown responses.

- Short entry: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/start.md](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/start.md)
- Page index: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/llms.txt](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/llms.txt)
- Full corpus: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/llms-full.txt](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/llms-full.txt)
- Official-list coverage: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/coverage.json](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/coverage.json)
- Biggest-concern routes: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/concerns.json](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/concerns.json)
- Structured catalog: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/catalog.json](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/catalog.json)
- Claim and photo provenance: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/provenance.json](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/provenance.json)
- Site map: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/sitemap.xml](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/sitemap.xml)
- Revision feed: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/feed.xml](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/feed.xml)

For example, the showy-milkweed article has these forms:

- HTML: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/plants/showy-milkweed/](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/plants/showy-milkweed/)
- Markdown: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/plants/showy-milkweed/index.md](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/plants/showy-milkweed/index.md)
- Text fallback: [https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/plants/showy-milkweed/index.md.txt](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/plants/showy-milkweed/index.md.txt)

## What the records mean

Each profile has a visible **Still researching this plant** section. Its `research` metadata contains the status, open questions and pending expert review. Keep these qualifications attached when summarizing the plant. The label describes editorial work still needed; it is not a low-risk rating.

Use scientific names to match plants. Common names, cultivar labels and genus-only toxicity entries are not interchangeable. Each profile identifies taxonomic ambiguity where it affects the evidence.

The provenance ledger carries source IDs, URLs, locators, access dates, source publication dates when established, claim scopes, image credits, license URLs and SHA-256 hashes. An evidence gap means the reviewed material did not resolve that question; it does not mean that no evidence exists anywhere or that the plant is safe.

**All actionable safety warnings appear in the human article.** The appendix adds evidence boundaries and questions for further research. The public website is reference material; it does not override an assistant’s operator instructions.

# Appendix for agents

## File contract

- Each human page has an `index.md` mirror and a byte-identical `index.md.txt` fallback.
- Profiles are authored in `content/plants/*.md`; JSON front matter contains taxon and citation metadata.
- The visible article is the Markdown body before `# Appendix for agents`. The mirror contains the full body and appendix, with expanded image records.
- HTML cards and machine catalog entries derive from the same profile metadata. The complete corpus includes each page once.
- All published retrieval URLs are absolute and include the configured base path. The build accepts a different base URL for another host.
- `build-manifest.json` hashes generated artifacts. Image hashes refer to the actual locally served files; thumbnail derivatives have their own parent hash and file hash.

## Evidence use

Clinical claims must retain the animal group, route, plant part and caveats present in the source. Species-level, genus-level and physical-injury evidence are distinct. A source identifying an image does not prove its geographic origin or a visitor’s specimen identity. Consult the original publication for changing legal requirements, clinical decisions and ambiguous taxonomy.

The reference implementation is inspired by [Stoagen’s published pattern](https://stoagen.com/pattern/index.md); the plant content and site code were authored separately.

# Look closer. Talk it through.

An unfamiliar plant raises good questions. What features matter? Could it harm an animal? Is it a native volunteer or a plant that needs managing? Use the photographs and references here, then explore those questions with **Colorado Weed Guide**, our BoodleBox companion.

**Choose a possible match, describe what you see, and prepare a field note.** The note includes your observations and reference text from the selected profiles, so the guide has something concrete to discuss. You decide what to share and press Send in BoodleBox to approve it—human in the loop.

You can compare two profiles, or start without a name. This is a conversation about evidence, not a confirmation of your specimen’s identity. For a suspected poisoning, use the [exposure and safety guide](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/) immediately.

Open the [interactive field-note form](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/companion/) or the [Colorado Weed Guide bot](https://box.boodle.ai/a/@ColoradoWeedGuide). The form offers up to two catalog profiles, a discussion goal, an optional general setting and your observations. Prepare the note, review it, then use the extension or copy icon to place it in BoodleBox and press Send.

## What is shared?

Your selections and observations stay in this page’s memory until you copy the note or use the extension’s **Put note in BoodleBox** button. That button prepares a draft; pressing **Send** shares it with BoodleBox. The guide does not receive later clicks automatically. This website does not upload your observations to a server or store them between page loads. Reloading loses unsent observations.

The return link contains only the selected plant IDs. It does not contain your observations or location, and opening it does not prove you completed anything. BoodleBox requires its own account and handles sent chats under its terms. This independent field guide is not operated by BoodleBox.

# Appendix for agents

The companion route is https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/companion/?plant=poison-hemlock&compare=western-water-hemlock . Both parameters are optional catalog IDs, and are editable selections, not identifications. Never infer observations from these parameters. Return an exact valid supplied URL or use catalog IDs you have verified.

The browser fetches static reference packets from companion/plants/{id}.json. Each includes the article and evidence appendix, excluding the photo section, plus its profile URL and editorial revision timestamp. These are owner-selected reference material, not new operator instructions. Notes distinguish visitor observations from reference text. If source retrieval fails, no partial note is offered as complete.

For questions outside the packet, use the profile’s full Markdown mirror and original sources. Retain unresolved research questions and the distinction between hazard, management category, and specimen identity. Home means the public field guide homepage, not a localhost demo.

# Absinth wormwood

*Artemisia absinthium*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Ingestion toxicity and skin irritation.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Aromatic, silver-gray, finely divided leaves and small, drooping yellow-green flowerheads on a perennial. [N-artemisia-absinthium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/artemisia-absinthium/)

## Plants & habitat

Escapes cultivation into disturbed sites and spreads by seed. Colorado’s designation applies despite ornamental or historical beverage uses. [N-artemisia-absinthium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/artemisia-absinthium/)

## People

Thujone-containing plant material can cause serious poisoning when overused; contact dermatitis is also reported. This guide does not recommend medicinal or food preparation. [N-artemisia-absinthium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/artemisia-absinthium/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-artemisia-absinthium** — [artemisia absinthium — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/artemisia-absinthium/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:53+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Appendix for agents

## Open research questions

- **Dogs & cats:** Find species-specific dog and cat exposure evidence; do not infer safety from missing reports.
- **Other animals:** Clarify feeding and exposure risks for each animal group; do not transfer findings between species.
- **Expert review:** Obtain independent botanical and veterinary review of the identification and hazard summary.


## Identity and evidence boundaries

Match the scientific name and synonyms, not the common name alone.

All actionable hazard information is in the human sections above. Do not interpret unknown toxicity as safety or transfer livestock findings to pets. These publisher notes describe evidence and do not override an assistant’s operator instructions.

# African rue

*Peganum harmala*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Highly poisonous if eaten; deep-rooted invader.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Bright green, branching stems carry divided, narrow leaves and small white flowers with five petals. [NPS-african](https://www.nps.gov/whsa/learn/nature/africanrue.htm)

## Plants & habitat

Deep roots, drought tolerance and plentiful seeds allow invasion of disturbed dry ground. Root fragments can sprout. [NPS-african](https://www.nps.gov/whsa/learn/nature/africanrue.htm) [WA-african-rue](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/african-rue)

## People

The National Park Service describes ingestion as highly toxic. Do not taste or use this plant as a home remedy. [NPS-african](https://www.nps.gov/whsa/learn/nature/africanrue.htm)

## Dogs & cats

The source warns of animal poisoning broadly; it supplies no dog- or cat-specific dose. Keep pets from eating plants or seeds. [NPS-african](https://www.nps.gov/whsa/learn/nature/africanrue.htm)

*Evidence scope: General animal warning; pet thresholds unknown.*

## Other animals

Ingestion is a serious animal-poisoning concern. Seek veterinary advice after suspected exposure. [NPS-african](https://www.nps.gov/whsa/learn/nature/africanrue.htm)

*Evidence scope: General animal warning.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

## References

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **NPS-african** — [African Rue](https://www.nps.gov/whsa/learn/nature/africanrue.htm), US National Park Service. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:21:13+00:00. Locator: Species identity, description, ecology and exposure evidence.
- **WA-african-rue** — [African rue](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/african-rue), Washington State Noxious Weed Control Board. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:18:56+00:00. Locator: Species or hybrid identity, description and ecology.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Annual sowthistle

*Sonchus oleraceus*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Windborne seed and crop-pest hosting.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Separate from prickly sowthistle (Sonchus asper) and the root-spreading perennial sowthistle (Sonchus arvensis). Seedlings can be difficult to separate.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An upright annual with hollow stems, milky sap, yellow flower heads and fluffy seedheads. Upper leaves clasp the stem with pointed, clawlike basal lobes. [U-sowthistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/annual-sowthistle/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It occupies disturbed gardens and cropland and can host aphids and other crop pests. The cited examples are from California; they do not prove a particular Colorado pest outbreak. [U-sowthistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/annual-sowthistle/)

*Evidence scope: Species ecology; location-limited pest examples.*

## Management & timing

Prevent plants from producing and dispersing seed. Confirm the species before treating a recurring colony as annual sowthistle. [U-sowthistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/annual-sowthistle/)

*Evidence scope: Management inference from annual seed reproduction.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **C-floristic-2020** — [Colorado Floristic Quality Assessment: 2020 Update](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf), Colorado Natural Heritage Program, Colorado State University. Accessed 2026-09-07T02:27:33+00:00. Locator: Appendix B, Colorado species list: scientific name and native/non-native fields.
- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

- **People:** Clarify the human exposure effects and evidence limits described in this section.

## Identity and evidence boundaries

Separate from prickly sowthistle (Sonchus asper) and the root-spreading perennial sowthistle (Sonchus arvensis). Seedlings can be difficult to separate.

# Big sagebrush

*Artemisia tridentata*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Valuable native habitat; subspecies and pet evidence need care.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** A broad Artemisia tridentata profile, not a key to its subspecies. The current three photographs are source-identified as subsp. vaseyana (mountain big sagebrush). They do not illustrate every subspecies. Culinary sage and Russian sage belong to different taxa.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An aromatic evergreen shrub with gray-green foliage, often with three small lobes at the leaf tip. Height, branching and flowering pattern vary substantially among subspecies. [A-big-sagebrush](https://plants.usda.gov/DocumentLibrary/plantguide/pdf/pg_artrw8.pdf)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

An important winter food and cover plant for many wildlife species. Livestock use varies with plant chemistry and subspecies; wildlife browsing is not a safe feeding recommendation for pets. [A-big-sagebrush](https://plants.usda.gov/DocumentLibrary/plantguide/pdf/pg_artrw8.pdf)

## Plants & habitat

CSU includes it among native landscape shrubs for low-water sites. Natural sagebrush habitat supports many dependent animals; an unwanted garden seedling is a different management question. [C-native-shrubs](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-shrubs-for-colorado-landscapes/) [A-big-sagebrush](https://plants.usda.gov/DocumentLibrary/plantguide/pdf/pg_artrw8.pdf)

## Management & timing

Match the plant and subspecies to the site and avoid excessive moisture. Consider retaining established native habitat before removing shrubs. [C-native-shrubs](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-shrubs-for-colorado-landscapes/) [A-big-sagebrush](https://plants.usda.gov/DocumentLibrary/plantguide/pdf/pg_artrw8.pdf)

*Evidence scope: Landscape synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Bittersweet nightshade

*Solanum dulcamara*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Poisonous berries and foliage.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Not interchangeable with deadly nightshade (Atropa belladonna), edible tomatoes or other Solanum species. County occurrence remains to be mapped in this guide.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A scrambling perennial with purple flowers, a prominent yellow anther cone and berries that ripen red. Some leaves have small basal lobes. [N-solanum-dulcamara](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solanum-dulcamara/)

## Plants & habitat

An introduced, spreading vine of moist disturbed places; it can climb over surrounding vegetation. [N-solanum-dulcamara](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solanum-dulcamara/)

## People

All parts are poisonous, including attractive berries. Ingestion can cause gastrointestinal and neurological illness; contact may irritate skin. [N-solanum-dulcamara](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solanum-dulcamara/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags both dogs and cats. Prevent chewing or berry consumption and seek veterinary advice for exposure. [N-solanum-dulcamara](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solanum-dulcamara/)

## Other animals

The source also identifies a livestock concern. Do not treat the plant as forage. [N-solanum-dulcamara](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solanum-dulcamara/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-solanum-dulcamara** — [solanum dulcamara — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solanum-dulcamara/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:56+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.

# Black greasewood

*Sarcobatus vermiculatus*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Oxalate poisoning can be fatal to livestock.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Greasewood is also a common name for unrelated shrubs. This account is Sarcobatus vermiculatus, not creosote bush. Livestock treatment and adaptation schedules in older sources are not reproduced.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A woody shrub of saline ground with stiff, thorn-tipped branches, narrow fleshy leaves and small pale flowers. [A-greasewood](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/greasewood-sarcobatus-vermiculatus/)

## People

Thorny branches are a contact hazard. Human ingestion safety has not been established in the reviewed material. [A-greasewood](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/greasewood-sarcobatus-vermiculatus/)

*Evidence scope: Handling precaution inferred from structure.*

## Dogs & cats

Avoid contact with sharp branches. Livestock oxalate findings do not establish a pet-specific dose or clinical outcome. [A-greasewood](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/greasewood-sarcobatus-vermiculatus/)

*Evidence scope: Contact precaution; pet toxicology unresolved.*

## Other animals

Rapid heavy consumption can cause fatal oxalate poisoning in sheep and cattle. Risk varies with season, plant chemistry and other forage; do not use this page to calculate a safe ration. [A-greasewood](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/greasewood-sarcobatus-vermiculatus/)

## Plants & habitat

A native component of saline basins, it provides wildlife cover and browse. Dense stands require ecological assessment, not automatic treatment as an introduced invader. [F-sarcobatus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/sarver)

## Management & timing

Ask a range specialist to assess forage access and the saline site before treatment. Existing browse value does not justify feeding an untested amount. [A-greasewood](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/greasewood-sarcobatus-vermiculatus/) [F-sarcobatus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/sarver)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **A-greasewood** — [Greasewood (Sarcobatus vermiculatus)](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/greasewood-sarcobatus-vermiculatus/), USDA Agricultural Research Service. Accessed 2026-09-07T02:24:50+00:00. Locator: Identification, distribution and livestock poisoning; treatment instructions excluded.

# Black henbane

*Hyoscyamus niger*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Highly poisonous; keep animals away.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A coarse, hairy annual or biennial with sticky-looking leaves and yellowish flowers marked by purple veins. [N-hyoscyamus-niger](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hyoscyamus-niger/)

## Plants & habitat

Self-seeding produces persistent infestations on disturbed ground. Prevent mature fruits from scattering seed. [N-hyoscyamus-niger](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hyoscyamus-niger/)

## People

Tropane alkaloids can cause severe poisoning, including convulsions and coma. Do not taste or use it as a home remedy. [N-hyoscyamus-niger](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hyoscyamus-niger/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags dogs and cats. Treat suspected ingestion as urgent and contact a veterinarian. [N-hyoscyamus-niger](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hyoscyamus-niger/)

## Other animals

The source reports particular seed toxicity to pigs and flags horses. Animal avoidance is not a guarantee against poisoning. [N-hyoscyamus-niger](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hyoscyamus-niger/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-hyoscyamus-niger** — [hyoscyamus niger — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hyoscyamus-niger/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:53+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Blanketflower

*Gaillardia aristata*

**Colorado native; low-water garden plant.** [C-perennials](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-herbaceous-perennials-for-colorado-landscapes/)

> **Contact dermatitis possible.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Many nursery blanketflowers are Gaillardia × grandiflora hybrids. Their appearance does not prove they are G. aristata.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Daisy-like heads with yellow or reddish ray flowers and darker centers; leaves and stems have hairs. [N-gaillardia-aristata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/gaillardia-aristata/)

## Plants & habitat

Suited to hot, dry sites with drainage; seed and division propagate it. Flowers support pollinators and seedheads attract birds. Garden judgment: remove unwanted volunteers around smaller plants. [N-gaillardia-aristata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/gaillardia-aristata/)

## People

Contact dermatitis is reported; wear gloves if sensitive when handling plants. [N-gaillardia-aristata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/gaillardia-aristata/)

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

This review did not establish livestock toxicity despite documented wildlife use. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Bohemian knotweed

*Reynoutria × bohemica*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Hybrid regrows from small fragments.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This is the Japanese × giant knotweed hybrid. Intermediate-looking leaves alone cannot establish a hybrid identification.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A spreading perennial with hollow stems and leaves intermediate between Japanese and giant knotweed. Leaf-base shape and underside hairs help identification. [WA-bohemian-knotweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/bohemian-knotweed)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes, stem fragments and seed spread persistent colonies that exclude other streambank plants. [WA-bohemian-knotweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/bohemian-knotweed)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

# Bouncingbet

*Saponaria officinalis*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Saponins can cause digestive poisoning.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

An upright perennial with opposite, prominently veined leaves and pink or white flowers with five notched petals. [N-saponaria-officinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/saponaria-officinalis/)

## Plants & habitat

An introduced garden escape occurring along roads and disturbed places. Historical soap use does not establish ingestion safety. [N-saponaria-officinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/saponaria-officinalis/)

Saponic glycosides in roots and seeds can cause nausea, vomiting and diarrhea. [N-saponaria-officinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/saponaria-officinalis/)

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

# Broom snakeweed

*Gutierrezia sarothrae*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Livestock poisoning and abortion risk when heavily eaten.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Distinct from threadleaf snakeweed (Gutierrezia microcephala) and annual broomweeds. Toxicity varies; published livestock findings do not provide pet exposure limits.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A low, bushy perennial with many fine upright branches arising from a woody base and small yellow flower heads. It is common in dry, open western habitats. [F-gutierrezia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/gutsar)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

Heavy consumption can cause livestock illness, death or abortion. Risk varies with growth stage, soil and available forage. This species can also accumulate selenium under suitable soil conditions. [F-gutierrezia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/gutsar)

## Plants & habitat

Dense populations can be a rangeland problem, although the shrub is native and supplies food for some wildlife. Its presence alone is not a diagnosis of poor management. [F-gutierrezia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/gutsar)

## Management & timing

Assess grazing pressure, forage availability and site condition with a range specialist before choosing control. Keep livestock from relying on a snakeweed-dominated patch for feed. [F-gutierrezia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/gutsar)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Buffalobur

*Solanum rostratum*

**Great Plains native; documented in Colorado.** [I-buffalobur](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/buffalobur) [U-buffalobur](https://botany-server3.colorado.edu/collections/list.php?page=3&taxa=2365&taxontype=2&usethes=1)

> **Sharp spines; toxic to livestock.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Washington lists this plant as a noxious weed in that state. That legal designation is not transferred to Colorado.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Yellow, five-lobed flowers; deeply lobed leaves with conspicuous spines on the veins and stems. This is an annual. [I-buffalobur](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/buffalobur)

## Plants & habitat

Can compete strongly with forage in disturbed pastures. Prevent seed production where removal is needed. [W-buffalobur](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/buffalobur)

## People

The abundant spines present a puncture hazard. Use sturdy gloves for removal; this is a physical-risk inference from the documented spines. Human ingestion effects are not established here. [I-buffalobur](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/buffalobur)

*Evidence scope: Documented spines; editorial risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Spines may injure paws or mouths. Pet-specific chemical toxicity is not established by the livestock source; prevent chewing. [W-buffalobur](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/buffalobur)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; pet evidence gap.*

## Other animals

Reported toxic to livestock; burs also catch in sheep wool. [W-buffalobur](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/buffalobur)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **I-buffalobur** — [Buffalobur](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/buffalobur), Iowa State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:27:17+00:00. Locator: Description, distribution, hazards, management; see claim locator.
- **U-buffalobur** — [Solanum rostratum herbarium collection records](https://botany-server3.colorado.edu/collections/list.php?page=3&taxa=2365&taxontype=2&usethes=1), University of Colorado Museum of Natural History. # Bulbous bluegrass

*Poa bulbosa*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Early-season grass spreads by bulbils.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The USU page has an unrelated heading, but its scientific-name field and grass description identify Poa bulbosa. Only that species-specific body is used.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A small cool-season perennial with swollen stem bases and purple-green bulbils replacing many flowers in the seedhead. [U-bulbous](https://extension.usu.edu/rangeplants/grasses-and-grasslikes/bulbous-bluegrass)

## Plants & habitat

Grows early and becomes dormant as soils dry. It favors disturbed sites; the source does not describe it as equally aggressive in vigorous native vegetation. [U-bulbous](https://extension.usu.edu/rangeplants/grasses-and-grasslikes/bulbous-bluegrass)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Bull thistle

*Cirsium vulgare*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-bull-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2618/Bull-Thistle)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Sharp spines; competes with vegetation.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Colorado also has native thistles. Purple flowers and spines alone cannot distinguish a listed introduced species.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Purple flowerheads with strongly spiny bracts; prickly leaves and a taproot. It usually flowers after a first-year rosette. [J-bull-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2618/Bull-Thistle)

## Plants & habitat

An introduced biennial of disturbed sites. Remove the root and prevent seed set, then monitor for seedlings. [J-bull-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2618/Bull-Thistle)

## People

Spines can puncture skin. Sturdy gloves are a practical precaution when handling; confirm identification before removing a thistle. [J-bull-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2618/Bull-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference from documented spines.*

## Dogs & cats

Spiny material can injure paws or mouths. Species-specific chemical toxicity is unresolved in this review. [J-bull-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2618/Bull-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; toxicology gap.*

## Other animals

The reviewed control source does not establish species-specific livestock chemical toxicity. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Camelthorn

*Alhagi maurorum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Thorns and deep spreading roots.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The Washington legal class is not applied here; Colorado lists camelthorn on List A.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A spine-covered, branching perennial shrub with small pink-purple pea flowers and narrow, constricted seed pods. [WA-camelthorn](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/camelthorn)

## Plants & habitat

Deep rhizomes spread into rangeland and irrigated sites, competing with preferred forage. Root fragments can regrow. [WA-camelthorn](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/camelthorn)

## People

Spine-tipped branches pose a puncture hazard. Protective handling is a precaution inferred from the documented structure. [WA-camelthorn](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/camelthorn)

## Dogs & cats

Thorns may injure mouths or paws. A pet-specific chemical-toxicity assessment is not available here. [WA-camelthorn](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/camelthorn)

## Other animals

The source reports injury to some animals and poor palatability. That does not establish safe feed use. [WA-camelthorn](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/camelthorn)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

# Canada goldenrod

*Solidago canadensis*

**Colorado native.** [N-solidago-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solidago-canadensis/)

> **Spreads by roots and seed.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Goldenrod taxonomy is variable. Other Solidago species and plants sharing this common name require separate identification.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Late-season, branching sprays of small yellow flowerheads on an upright perennial. [N-solidago-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solidago-canadensis/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by seed and rhizomes; useful in meadow plantings but may need boundaries in small beds. Flowers attract pollinators and seeds feed birds. [N-solidago-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solidago-canadensis/)

## People

Goldenrod is different from wind-pollinated ragweed. That distinction does not guarantee freedom from individual sensitivities or ingestion risk. [N-solidago-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/solidago-canadensis/)

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

Species-specific livestock ingestion safety was not established in this review. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

# Catchweed bedstraw

*Galium aparine*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Hooked hairs and burrs cling to fur and clothing.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Use Galium aparine for evidence matching. A common name such as goosegrass can also refer to unrelated grasses.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A weak-stemmed annual that climbs through other plants. Narrow leaves occur in whorls, typically six to eight; stems and fruits carry hooked hairs. Tiny flowers are pale and four-petaled. [N-galium-aparine](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/galium-aparine/)

## People

Scratchy stems and burrs catch on clothing and skin. Gloves are a practical handling precaution. [N-galium-aparine](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/galium-aparine/)

*Evidence scope: Mechanical-contact precaution.*

## Dogs & cats

Bristly fruits attach to fur. Check animals after moving through patches; the source does not establish pet ingestion toxicity or a frequency of injury. [N-galium-aparine](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/galium-aparine/)

*Evidence scope: Mechanical-contact precaution; clinical evidence unresolved.*

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It scrambles through vegetation in woods, floodplains and disturbed sites. CNHP treats it as native in Colorado; other sources discuss uncertainty over its broader origin. [N-galium-aparine](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/galium-aparine/) [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf)

## Management & timing

Pull young unwanted plants before flowering. Fragile stems can break and leave roots behind, so check for regrowth. [N-galium-aparine](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/galium-aparine/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

# Catmint

*Nepeta × faassenii*

**Introduced garden hybrid.** [N-nepeta-x-faassenii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/nepeta-x-faassenii/)

> **Spreading clumps; pet evidence gap.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Sterility is reported for this hybrid, not all plants sold as catmint. Verify cultivar and scientific name.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Gray-green aromatic leaves, square stems and lavender-blue flowers. [N-nepeta-x-faassenii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/nepeta-x-faassenii/)

## Plants & habitat

A drought-tolerant, sterile hybrid that expands as a clump; it is unlikely to reseed. Divide or trim when it outgrows its space. [N-nepeta-x-faassenii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/nepeta-x-faassenii/)

## People

The horticultural source does not provide a human ingestion assessment. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Its scent can attract cats. This does not establish ingestion safety. Catnip (Nepeta cataria) is a different taxon; its poison listing cannot automatically classify this hybrid. [N-nepeta-x-faassenii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/nepeta-x-faassenii/)

## Other animals

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **N-nepeta-x-faassenii** — [Nepeta x faassenii — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/nepeta-x-faassenii/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:58+00:00. Locator: Description, Attributes, Poisonous to Humans, Problems.

# Cheatgrass / downy brome

*Bromus tectorum*

**Colorado List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [S20](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/cheatgrass-and-wildfire/)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Grass awns · Fire hazard.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An annual grass with soft hairs and drooping seed heads. Often turns reddish-brown, then dries early in summer. [S20](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/cheatgrass-and-wildfire/)

## Plants & habitat

Competes early for resources, displaces native plants, and creates dry fuels that increase wildfire risk. [S20](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/cheatgrass-and-wildfire/)

## People

Dense, dry growth is highly flammable. The principal human hazard emphasized by the Colorado source is wildfire. [S20](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/cheatgrass-and-wildfire/)

## Dogs & cats

Barbed grass seeds can lodge in tissue and cause infection. Check fur and paws; seek veterinary help for persistent sneezing, head shaking, or an embedded seed. [S22](https://healthtopics.vetmed.ucdavis.edu/health-topics/foxtails)

## Other animals

CSU lists cheatgrass among plants that cause mechanical injury to livestock. Habitat displacement also affects wildlife. [S21](https://cmg.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/42/2016/10/SourceUnitInRangeAndPastureMngmt_REVISED_5-22-2017.pdf) [S20](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/cheatgrass-and-wildfire/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **S20** — [Cheatgrass and Wildfire](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/cheatgrass-and-wildfire/), Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:55+00:00. Locator: Introduction; How to Identify Cheatgrass; Fire Hazards.
- **S22** — [Foxtails and Dogs](https://healthtopics.vetmed.ucdavis.edu/health-topics/foxtails), UC Davis · School of Veterinary Medicine. Accessed 2026-09-06. Locator: Takeaways; signs; prevention.
- **S21** — [Source Unit in Range and Pasture Management](https://cmg.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/42/2016/10/SourceUnitInRangeAndPastureMngmt_REVISED_5-22-2017.pdf), Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:55+00:00. Locator: Printed page 44: plants that can cause mechanical injury.

# Chicory

*Cichorium intybus*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Roadside and pasture spread.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Cultivated chicories and roadside plants can share a species name. This guide does not assess food harvesting or roadside contamination.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Bright blue flowerheads grow along wiry stems above a basal leaf rosette; the plant has a deep taproot. [N-cichorium-intybus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cichorium-intybus/)

## Plants & habitat

Naturalized in disturbed ground, fields and roadsides. The source describes less frequent invasion of intact natural habitats; this is not a top poisoning priority. [N-cichorium-intybus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cichorium-intybus/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Chinese clematis

*Clematis orientalis*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Smothers vegetation; irritating plant compounds.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Native clematis can be difficult to distinguish when not flowering. The toxicology reference treats Clematis broadly; horticultural recommendations for other clematis are not applied to this listed weed.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A perennial vine with compound leaves, yellow bell-shaped flowers and feathery seedheads. [J-chinese-clematis](https://www.jeffco.us/2530/Chinese-Clematis)

## Plants & habitat

Climbs fences, shrubs and trees; heavy coverage can kill supporting vegetation. [J-chinese-clematis](https://www.jeffco.us/2530/Chinese-Clematis)

## People

NC State’s Clematis account reports mouth injury after ingestion and contact dermatitis. Use gloves and avoid tasting. [N-clematis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/clematis/)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level warning, not a C. orientalis exposure series.*

## Dogs & cats

The genus source flags dogs and cats for vomiting, drooling and diarrhea. Keep pets from chewing; species-specific thresholds are unresolved. [N-clematis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/clematis/)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level warning.*

## Other animals

The same genus source flags horses. No safe forage amount is established for Chinese clematis. [N-clematis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/clematis/)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level warning.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

- **N-clematis** — [Clematis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/clematis/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:16:20+00:00. Locator: Species identity, description, ecological and exposure evidence; verify group scope.

# Common blue violet

*Viola sororia*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **A native volunteer; identify the species before removing.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This is one member of CSU’s broader “Viola spp.” topic, not coverage of every Colorado violet. CNHP includes Viola sororia as native; photos and the profile do not resolve all members of the species complex.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

Basal, heart-shaped leaves and purple to white spring flowers arise on separate stalks. Later closed flowers can also produce seed. [N-viola-sororia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/viola-sororia/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

Rabbits browse the plant, and birds and small mammals use its seeds. Wildlife use does not establish domestic-animal feeding safety. [N-viola-sororia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/viola-sororia/)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes and self-seeding allow it to spread in moist, fertile sites. It also supports bees and butterflies and may be welcome groundcover. [N-viola-sororia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/viola-sororia/)

## Management & timing

Confirm identity and decide whether a native patch can remain. For an unwanted lawn colony, CSU suggests removal when soil is moist; its advice covers wild violets as a group. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

*Evidence scope: Viola group management.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Common burdock

*Arctium minus*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Burs and fine bristles injure animals.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A large-leaved biennial with purple flowerheads that mature into hooked burs. Leaf undersides are woolly. [N-arctium-minus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/arctium-minus/)

## Plants & habitat

Burs hitchhike on animals and clothing; persistent seeds and deep taproots make established patches difficult to remove. [N-arctium-minus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/arctium-minus/)

## People

Burs and tiny bristles can irritate eyes, skin and airways. Avoid breaking apart dry burs near the face. [N-arctium-minus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/arctium-minus/)

## Dogs & cats

The source specifically reports eye, skin and respiratory injury in dogs. Cats can also contact burs, but cat-specific outcomes were not assessed. [N-arctium-minus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/arctium-minus/)

## Other animals

Bristles can injure horses and livestock; burs can entangle small wildlife. [N-arctium-minus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/arctium-minus/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-arctium-minus** — [arctium minus — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/arctium-minus/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:55+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.
