# Common chickweed

*Stellaria media*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **A cool-season weed that can seed while still small.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Common chickweed is not every plant called chickweed. Species identification and contamination history must be established independently of any food-use claim.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A soft, spreading annual with opposite oval leaves and small white flowers. Five deeply split petals look like ten. A line of hairs runs along the stems. [N-stellaria-media](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/stellaria-media/)

## People

The source reports saponins in the leaves alongside food uses. This profile does not establish a safe portion or identify a plant for foraging. [N-stellaria-media](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/stellaria-media/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It can root at stem joints and reseed quickly in moist, fertile ground. It also provides food and habitat for insects and birds. [N-stellaria-media](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/stellaria-media/)

## Management & timing

Look for small plants during cool weather. Shallow roots make young patches relatively easy to pull; remove them before further seed production. [N-stellaria-media](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/stellaria-media/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Common cocklebur

*Xanthium strumarium*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Burs catch in fur; seedlings and seeds are hazardous.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Xanthium strumarium is treated broadly by some sources and split into other taxa by others. Photographs retain their original source identifications. Native status and a local specimen’s exact taxon need separate review.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A summer annual with rough, broad leaves and hard burs covered in hooked prickles. [N-xanthium-strumarium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/xanthium-strumarium/)

## Plants & habitat

Can form colonies in fields and wet disturbed ground. Hooked burs move on animals and clothing. [N-xanthium-strumarium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/xanthium-strumarium/)

## People

NC State flags human poisoning but provides primarily animal clinical descriptions. Do not infer a human dose or identical symptoms. [N-xanthium-strumarium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/xanthium-strumarium/)

*Evidence scope: Source warning; human clinical detail limited.*

## Dogs & cats

Burs attach to fur and can cause contact problems. Animal ingestion warnings do not establish dog- or cat-specific thresholds; prevent chewing seedlings, seeds and burs. [N-xanthium-strumarium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/xanthium-strumarium/)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference and general animal warning.*

## Other animals

Seedling cotyledons and seeds are the principal ingestion concern. The source describes low blood sugar, weakness, spasms and potentially fatal poisoning. [N-xanthium-strumarium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/xanthium-strumarium/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-xanthium-strumarium** — [xanthium strumarium — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/xanthium-strumarium/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:56+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Common crupina

*Crupina vulgaris*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Dense stands reduce forage.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Washington source used for biology; Colorado’s current rules supply the legal designation. “Not known toxic” in the source is not a feeding-safety assessment.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A winter annual with finely divided, bristly leaves and narrow, vase-shaped purple flowerheads. [WA-common-crupina](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/common-crupina)

## Plants & habitat

Reproduces from seed and can form dense stands that reduce available livestock forage. [WA-common-crupina](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/common-crupina)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

# Common dandelion

*Taraxacum officinale*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Persistent lawn volunteer; sap can irritate skin.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Used in the common broad sense (T. officinale aggregate / section Ruderalia). Photographs are not intended to resolve small segregate taxa or Colorado’s native dandelions.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A basal rosette of toothed leaves, single yellow heads on hollow leafless stalks, and a rounded puffball of windborne seeds. [N-taraxacum-officinale](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/taraxacum-officinale/)

## Plants & habitat

A familiar Colorado lawn plant with a persistent taproot. Flowers provide early pollen and nectar; removal is a site choice, not a claim that it has no ecological value. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/) [N-taraxacum-officinale](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/taraxacum-officinale/)

## People

Sap can cause contact dermatitis in susceptible people. Recorded food uses do not establish that an unidentified or treated lawn plant is safe to eat. [N-taraxacum-officinale](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/taraxacum-officinale/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

# Common lambsquarters

*Chenopodium album*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

**Identification caveat:** Chenopodium album is distinct from netseed lambsquarters (C. berlandieri). Exact native status within this variable complex is not resolved here.

An annual with variable goosefoot-shaped leaves, a whitish mealy coating on young growth and small green flower clusters. [N-chenopodium-album](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/chenopodium-album/)

## Plants & habitat

Pueblo County Extension identifies C. album among common Colorado weeds. Many persistent seeds allow repeated emergence after disturbance. [C-edible-weeds](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/early-spring-edible-plants-in-your-yard-and-garden/) [N-chenopodium-album](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/chenopodium-album/)

NC State reports digestive and nervous-system effects after large ingestion and notes oxalic acid and saponins. Food-use records are not a safe-dose recommendation. [N-chenopodium-album](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/chenopodium-album/)

*Malva neglecta*

Low spreading stems, rounded shallowly lobed leaves and small pale flowers with notched petals; fruits resemble little cheese wheels. [N-malva-neglecta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/malva-neglecta/) [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

Common in disturbed Colorado gardens and thin lawns. Taproots become difficult to remove, and durable seeds support recurring patches. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/) [N-malva-neglecta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/malva-neglecta/)

# Common mullein

*Verbascum thapsus*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Dense rosettes and persistent volunteers.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Soft leaves do not establish medicinal or ingestion safety. This is not lamb’s-ear (Stachys byzantina).

A woolly leaf rosette produces a tall, dense spike of yellow flowers, typically in its second year. [N-verbascum-thapsus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/verbascum-thapsus/)

## Plants & habitat

Self-seeds in dry, disturbed places and can crowd neighboring vegetation. Prevent seed formation where control is needed. [N-verbascum-thapsus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/verbascum-thapsus/)

# Common purslane

*Portulaca oleracea*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Pet ingestion hazard despite human food uses.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Distinguish succulent purslane from low-growing spurges. The ASPCA entry is Portulaca oleracea, not merely an ornamental plant sharing a common name.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Low, spreading red-tinged stems, fleshy leaves and small yellow flowers. [N-portulaca-oleracea](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/portulaca-oleracea/)

## Plants & habitat

A familiar Colorado garden weed. Seeds and surviving stem pieces can sustain a patch. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/) [N-portulaca-oleracea](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/portulaca-oleracea/)

## People

The species has documented human food uses, but this guide provides neither a foraging identification nor a safe serving amount. Do not transfer food-use claims to pets. [N-portulaca-oleracea](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/portulaca-oleracea/)

*Evidence scope: Food-use record, not individual safety assessment.*

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists this exact species as toxic to dogs and cats because of soluble oxalates. Contact a veterinarian after suspected ingestion. [A-purslane](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/purslane)

## Other animals

ASPCA also lists horses. No safe forage amount is established here. [A-purslane](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/purslane)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Common ragweed

*Ambrosia artemisiifolia*

**Colorado native.** [N-ambrosia-artemisiifolia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ambrosia-artemisiifolia/)

> **Pollen allergy; skin irritation.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An annual with deeply divided, fern-like leaves and inconspicuous green flower spikes. [N-ambrosia-artemisiifolia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ambrosia-artemisiifolia/)

## Plants & habitat

A dry-site volunteer that produces many persistent seeds; birds eat its seeds. Remove unwanted plants before flowering and seed set. [N-ambrosia-artemisiifolia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ambrosia-artemisiifolia/)

## People

Windborne pollen is a major cause of hay fever; contact dermatitis is also reported. [N-ambrosia-artemisiifolia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ambrosia-artemisiifolia/)

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

This review did not establish species-specific livestock toxicity. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Common St. Johnswort

*Hypericum perforatum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Photosensitivity and animal exposure concerns.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Yellow five-petaled flowers with many stamens; small opposite leaves show translucent dots when backlit. [N-hypericum-perforatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hypericum-perforatum/)

## Plants & habitat

Seeds and spreading underground stems create persistent patches that can displace native plants. [N-hypericum-perforatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hypericum-perforatum/)

## People

Hypericin-containing plant material can cause photosensitivity and dermatitis. The guide does not assess herbal use or medication interactions. [N-hypericum-perforatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hypericum-perforatum/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags dogs and cats. This source does not establish a pet-specific safe amount. [N-hypericum-perforatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hypericum-perforatum/)

## Other animals

Horses are also flagged by the source. Seek veterinary advice before allowing access to contaminated forage. [N-hypericum-perforatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hypericum-perforatum/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Common sunflower

*Helianthus annuus*

**Colorado native.** [S28](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/loving-our-native-plants/) [S23](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/helianthus-annuus/)

> **Listed non-toxic to dogs & cats.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An annual with rough, hairy stems and yellow flower rays around darker central disks. [S23](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/helianthus-annuus/)

## Plants & habitat

Readily self-seeds and can become weedy. Garden judgment: thin unwanted volunteers where they crowd other plants. [S23](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/helianthus-annuus/)

## People

Human ingestion safety is not evaluated in this guide. This profile is not a recommendation to forage.

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA explicitly lists common sunflower as non-toxic to dogs and cats. Any plant material can still upset a pet’s stomach. [S24](https://www.aspca.org/news/plants-support-your-local-wildlife) [S25](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants)

## Other animals

Seeds feed birds and squirrels. Livestock safety is not established by the dog-and-cat source. [S23](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/helianthus-annuus/) [S24](https://www.aspca.org/news/plants-support-your-local-wildlife)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Common tansy

*Tanacetum vulgare*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Toxic ingestion; contact dermatitis.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Common tansy is not tansy ragwort (Jacobaea vulgaris). Their chemical hazards and flower structures differ.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Fern-like leaves and flat clusters of yellow, button-like flowerheads without showy ray petals. [N-tanacetum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/tanacetum-vulgare/)

## Plants & habitat

Seeds, rhizomes and root fragments build dense stands that displace other plants and degrade pastures. [N-tanacetum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/tanacetum-vulgare/)

## People

Thujone can cause serious poisoning, including convulsions, and the plant can irritate skin. Avoid teas and home remedies. [N-tanacetum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/tanacetum-vulgare/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Toxicity is reported for horses and cattle. Specialist grazing by other animals is not a general feeding recommendation. [N-tanacetum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/tanacetum-vulgare/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-tanacetum-vulgare** — [tanacetum vulgare — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/tanacetum-vulgare/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:53+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Common teasel

*Dipsacus fullonum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-teasel](https://www.jeffco.us/2152/Teasel)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Prickly heads; prolific seed spread.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The Colorado county designation is for Dipsacus spp. This page illustrates source-identified D. fullonum; cutleaf teasel, D. laciniatus, requires a separate identification.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Prickly oval heads above stems with paired leaves that form cups. The county’s teasel account describes purple or white flowers and curved bracts. [J-teasel](https://www.jeffco.us/2152/Teasel)

*Evidence scope: Dipsacus group; source-identified photo examples.*

## Plants & habitat

Introduced teasels spread by seed in disturbed areas and ditch banks. Remove rosettes or flowering heads before seed dispersal and monitor for new plants. [J-teasel](https://www.jeffco.us/2152/Teasel)

*Evidence scope: Dipsacus group.*

## People

Prickly heads and stems present a handling hazard; gloves are a practical precaution. [J-teasel](https://www.jeffco.us/2152/Teasel)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Prickly dried material may injure paws or mouths. Chemical toxicity has not been established here. [J-teasel](https://www.jeffco.us/2152/Teasel)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; toxicity gap.*

## Other animals

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Common yarrow

*Achillea millefolium*

**Low-water garden plant.** [S03](https://www.extension.colostate.edu/docs/pubs/native/FrontRangeSm.pdf) [S07](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/achillea-millefolium/)

> **Toxic to pets · Skin irritation.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Finely divided, fernlike leaves and flat-topped clusters of small flowers. Native range includes North America, Europe, and Asia; cultivated forms vary. [S07](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/achillea-millefolium/)

## Plants & habitat

Tolerates drought and feeds pollinators, but can spread aggressively through underground stems and seed. [S07](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/achillea-millefolium/)

## People

Contact can cause dermatitis. Gloves reduce skin contact during garden work. [S07](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/achillea-millefolium/)

## Dogs & cats

Toxic to dogs and cats. Reported signs include vomiting, diarrhea, drooling, and dermatitis. [S08](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/yarrow)

## Other animals

Also toxic to horses. ASPCA includes colic among reported signs; do not infer safety for other livestock. [S08](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/yarrow)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **S03** — [Low-Water Native Plants for Colorado Gardens: Front Range & Foothills](https://www.extension.colostate.edu/docs/pubs/native/FrontRangeSm.pdf), Colorado Native Plant Society / CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:53+00:00. Locator: Plant table: Asclepias speciosa and Achillea millefolium.

# Creeping woodsorrel

*Oxalis corniculata*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Oxalate-containing plant; keep pets from grazing.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Oxalis corniculata is not a Trifolium clover or a Rumex sorrel. Do not attach an ASPCA Rumex scutatus entry to this plant merely because both are called sorrel.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

Three heart-shaped leaflets can resemble clover, but the flowers are yellow and seed capsules are narrow and cylindrical. Creeping stems spread across the ground. [N-oxalis-corniculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/oxalis-corniculata/)

## People

All parts have toxic potential from soluble oxalates. A sour taste or a reference to traditional food use is not a safe-amount recommendation. [N-oxalis-corniculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/oxalis-corniculata/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags this species as a problem for dogs and cats. Prevent repeated grazing; the reviewed entry does not quantify a safe amount or species-specific clinical outcome in pets. [N-oxalis-corniculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/oxalis-corniculata/)

## Other animals

The source reports colic and possible kidney injury after substantial ingestion of Oxalis by horses. [N-oxalis-corniculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/oxalis-corniculata/)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level horse evidence.*

## Plants & habitat

Ripe capsules eject seeds, and spreading stems can make established patches difficult to remove. [N-oxalis-corniculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/oxalis-corniculata/)

## Management & timing

Inspect incoming nursery plants and remove volunteers before capsules mature. Recheck for regrowth and seedlings. [N-oxalis-corniculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/oxalis-corniculata/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-oxalis-corniculata** — [Oxalis corniculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/oxalis-corniculata/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T02:23:11+00:00. Locator: Description, identification attributes and plant problems; toxicity section where present.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

Oxalis corniculata is not a Trifolium clover or a Rumex sorrel. Do not attach an ASPCA Rumex scutatus entry to this plant merely because both are called sorrel.

# Crownvetch

*Securigera varia*

**Introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Toxic to horses; spreading roots can smother vegetation.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Older NC State material uses Coronilla varia; the Colorado assessment uses Securigera varia. This is separate from Vicia species and their distinct feeding hazards.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A trailing perennial legume with many paired leaflets and rounded clusters of pink-and-white pea flowers. Unlike true vetches, it lacks tendrils. [N-securigera-varia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/securigera-varia/) [F-securigera](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/secvar)

## People

NC State lists poisonous characteristics and potentially serious ingestion effects. No safe amount is established. [N-securigera-varia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/securigera-varia/)

## Dogs & cats

Sources warn about nonruminants, but reviewed species-specific dog and cat clinical evidence is incomplete. Prevent chewing; cattle forage use cannot be treated as pet clearance. [N-securigera-varia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/securigera-varia/) [F-securigera](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/secvar)

*Evidence scope: Animal-group evidence; pet-specific gap.*

## Other animals

Crownvetch contains nitroglycosides toxic to horses. Its use as forage for some ruminants does not make it suitable horse feed. [F-securigera](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/secvar)

## Plants & habitat

It spreads by rhizomes and seed and can form a dense cover that displaces other plants. Former erosion-control use explains many naturalized colonies. [N-securigera-varia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/securigera-varia/)

## Management & timing

Avoid introducing it near natural habitat or horse forage. Established root systems need a sustained local control plan and monitoring. [N-securigera-varia](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/securigera-varia/) [F-securigera](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/secvar)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Curly dock

*Rumex crispus*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Soluble oxalates; persistent roots and seeds.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A basal rosette and upright stems with long, wavy-edged leaves; green flower clusters mature to brown seed structures. [N-rumex-crispus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rumex-crispus/)

## Plants & habitat

A Colorado weed of disturbed land. Its long-lived root and persistent seeds allow survival through mowing and changing moisture. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/) [N-rumex-crispus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rumex-crispus/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags dogs and cats and reports soluble oxalates, salivation, tremors and possible kidney injury; it describes kidney failure as rare in these pets. [N-rumex-crispus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rumex-crispus/)

## Other animals

The source flags horses, but does not establish a safe forage quantity. [N-rumex-crispus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rumex-crispus/)

*Evidence scope: Source hazard flag; limited horse clinical detail.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Curlycup gumweed

*Grindelia squarrosa*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Sticky resin and conditional selenium concern.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Grindelia squarrosa includes multiple varieties. Photographs and this profile do not constitute a variety-level key or a selenium test.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A branching, taprooted biennial or perennial with resinous foliage and flower heads. Growth form varies among the varieties grouped in this species. [F-grindelia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/grisqu)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

Sticky secretions are a practical grooming concern, inferred from reports of livestock coats becoming gummed. Pet ingestion toxicity is unresolved. [F-grindelia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/grisqu)

*Evidence scope: Contact precaution inferred from livestock observations.*

## Other animals

Usually unpalatable to livestock. Resin can gum coats, and the plant can absorb selenium when it is available; this does not mean every specimen contains a toxic concentration. [F-grindelia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/grisqu)

## Plants & habitat

It tolerates dry disturbed ground and may form dense patches on depleted range or roadsides. Native status and weediness depend on context. [F-grindelia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/grisqu)

## Management & timing

Investigate site condition before broad removal. For an unwanted small patch, prevent seed production and monitor replacement vegetation. [F-grindelia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/grisqu) [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Cutleaf teasel

*Dipsacus laciniatus*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Prickly stands can displace native vegetation.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Common teasel usually has unlobed leaves and lavender flowers. Flower color alone does not establish the species.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Deeply cut, opposite leaves clasp the stem; generally white flowers open on prickly, elongated heads. [F-teasels](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/dipspp)

## Plants & habitat

Reported near Denver. Dense stands can threaten meadow and wetland vegetation; impacts vary among sites. [F-teasels](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/dipspp)

## People

Prickles on stems and heads make unprotected handling a puncture concern. [F-teasels](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/dipspp)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Prickly plants present a contact hazard; the reviewed source does not establish pet ingestion effects. [F-teasels](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/dipspp)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; ingestion evidence gap.*

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Cypress spurge

*Euphorbia cyparissias*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Sap can injure skin and eyes.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A low perennial with crowded, needle-like leaves and yellow-green flower bracts. Do not snap stems to test for milky sap. [N-euphorbia-cyparissias](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-cyparissias/)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes and seed build dense colonies that can overrun neighboring vegetation. [N-euphorbia-cyparissias](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-cyparissias/)

## People

Latex can cause dermatitis, mouth injury and serious corneal damage. Protect skin and eyes during handling. [N-euphorbia-cyparissias](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-cyparissias/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags dogs and cats and advises keeping pets away. This is not a pet-specific dose assessment. [N-euphorbia-cyparissias](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-cyparissias/)

## Other animals

The source reports potential toxicity to cattle and horses. [N-euphorbia-cyparissias](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-cyparissias/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

# Dalmatian toadflax

*Linaria dalmatica*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-dalmatian-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2469/Dalmatian-Toadflax)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Spreads from roots; livestock concern.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Distinguish the two toadflaxes by leaves as well as flowers. A snapdragon-like bloom is not evidence of garden suitability.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

## Recognize

Yellow snapdragon-like flowers with long spurs; broad, waxy, heart-shaped leaves clasp the stems. [J-dalmatian-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2469/Dalmatian-Toadflax)

## Plants & habitat

A perennial spreading by roots and seed. Root fragments can form new plants; repeated casual digging or mowing is unlikely to solve an established patch. [J-dalmatian-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2469/Dalmatian-Toadflax)

## People

Human ingestion effects were not established in this review. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

The county reports possible livestock toxicity. Grazing is not recommended as a simple control method; pet safety cannot be inferred from this warning. [J-dalmatian-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2469/Dalmatian-Toadflax)

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. ## Plants & habitat

Dry plants break off and tumble, spreading seeds through disturbed rangeland and roadsides. [J-diffuse-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/2466/Diffuse-Knapweed)

## People

The county reports skin irritation. Wear gloves when handling plants. [J-diffuse-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/2466/Diffuse-Knapweed)

Spiny bracts present a physical contact concern. The reviewed source does not establish pet ingestion effects. [J-diffuse-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/2466/Diffuse-Knapweed)

# Elongated mustard

*Brassica elongata*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Early detection priority in dry disturbed land.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Reference photographs identify B. elongata subsp. integrifolia. Colorado lists B. elongata broadly. The fact sheet’s inconsistent sepal-count caption is not adopted.

## Recognize

An upright mustard with lance-shaped basal leaves, non-clasping upper leaves, yellow four-petaled flowers and slender seedpods. [CO-elongated-mustard](https://fremontcountyco.gov/sites/default/files/weed-control/weed-files/elongated-mustard-fact-sheet.pdf)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by seed along disturbed, rocky and semi-arid ground. Prompt reporting helps prevent establishment. [CO-elongated-mustard](https://fremontcountyco.gov/sites/default/files/weed-control/weed-files/elongated-mustard-fact-sheet.pdf)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

# Eurasian watermilfoil

*Myriophyllum spicatum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Fragments spread dense aquatic growth.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Native milfoils and hybrids can look similar. Confirm identification before management; a feathery leaf is not enough.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Submerged, feather-like leaves occur in whorls; small flowering spikes emerge above the water. [N-myriophyllum-spicatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/myriophyllum-spicatum/)

## Plants & habitat

Stem fragments spread readily. Dense growth crowds native plants and obstructs recreation and water infrastructure. [N-myriophyllum-spicatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/myriophyllum-spicatum/)

## People

Dense growth interferes with swimming and boating; avoid entering heavily vegetated water. [N-myriophyllum-spicatum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/myriophyllum-spicatum/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Fernleaf yarrow

*Achillea filipendulina*

**Introduced drought-tolerant perennial.** [N-achillea-filipendulina](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/achillea-filipendulina/)

> **Spreads; pet toxicity unresolved.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Do not merge this taxon with Achillea millefolium solely because both are called yarrow.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Tall stems hold flat yellow flower clusters above fern-like green leaves. [N-achillea-filipendulina](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/achillea-filipendulina/)

## Plants & habitat

Tolerates dry sites and can crowd its space; divide as needed and avoid overwatering. Cultivars differ in seed production. [N-achillea-filipendulina](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/achillea-filipendulina/)

## People

This species-specific horticultural source does not establish human ingestion safety. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

The ASPCA yarrow entry names Achillea millefolium. It does not resolve toxicity for A. filipendulina; prevent chewing while this gap remains. [S08](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/yarrow)

## Other animals

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Field bindweed

*Convolvulus arvensis*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Smothers plants; livestock concern.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Morning glory is an ambiguous common name. Ipomoea poison listings do not automatically apply to Convolvulus arvensis.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Twining or trailing vines with arrowhead leaves and white to pink funnel-shaped flowers. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Plants & habitat

Deep roots and persistent seed make control difficult. Vines overgrow neighboring plants; repeated shoot removal and a site-specific plan are usually needed. [N-convolvulus-arvensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/convolvulus-arvensis/) [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## People

This review did not establish human ingestion effects. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

Cornell reports alkaloids that can cause digestive problems in some livestock. Pet effects and a safe feeding threshold are not established here. [K-bindweed](https://blogs.cornell.edu/weedid/859-2/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Content revised: 2026-09-07T02:43:00Z

# Foxtail barley

*Hordeum jubatum*

**Colorado native.** [C-foxtail](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/foxtail-barley/)

> **Barbed seed awns injure animals.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A short-lived perennial bunchgrass with silky, often pink-tinged seedheads. It commonly occupies moist, disturbed or saline sites. [C-foxtail](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/foxtail-barley/)

## Plants & habitat

Can increase under heavy grazing as livestock avoid mature seedheads. Management should reduce seed production and support desirable grasses. [C-foxtail](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/foxtail-barley/)

## People

Sharp seed awns can catch on clothing; avoiding direct contact is a physical-risk precaution. This is not a chemical-poison diagnosis. [C-foxtail](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/foxtail-barley/)

*Evidence scope: Editorial physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Grass awns can lodge in tissue and migrate, causing serious injury. Avoid mature seedheads and seek veterinary assessment for suspected embedded awns. The pet source discusses foxtails broadly. [S22](https://healthtopics.vetmed.ucdavis.edu/health-topics/foxtails)

*Evidence scope: Grass-awn group; not a species-specific pet case series.*

## Other animals

Mature awns injure livestock mouths, noses and eyes. Contaminated hay is unsafe and should not be fed or sold. [C-foxtail](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/foxtail-barley/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **C-foxtail** — [Foxtail barley](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/foxtail-barley/), Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:24:02+00:00. Locator: Species entries.
- **S22** — [Foxtails and Dogs](https://healthtopics.vetmed.ucdavis.edu/health-topics/foxtails), UC Davis · School of Veterinary Medicine. Accessed 2026-09-06. Locator: Takeaways; signs; prevention.

*Artemisia frigida*

A low, aromatic, silvery native with finely divided foliage and a woody base. [F-fringed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/artfri)

## Plants & habitat

Forms spreading mats and produces seed. Garden judgment: use where low spreading growth fits, then manage volunteers. [F-fringed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/artfri)

Human ingestion safety was not established in the reviewed gardening and ecological sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

# Giant knotweed

*Reynoutria sachalinensis*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Very persistent riparian colonies.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The state rule uses Fallopia sachalinense. Giant, Japanese and Bohemian knotweeds overlap in appearance; confirm identity beyond plant height.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Very large, heart-based leaves on hollow, jointed stems; small greenish-white flower clusters arise near upper leaves. [WA-giant-knotweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/giant-knotweed)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes form dense colonies and even small fragments can regenerate. Keep cut material and contaminated soil from spreading. [WA-giant-knotweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/giant-knotweed)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Content revised: 2026-09-07T02:46:00Z

# Giant ragweed

*Ambrosia trifida*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Allergenic pollen and strong competition in disturbed ground.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Giant ragweed is Ambrosia trifida. A mismatched Latin name in the compared manufacturer guide is not adopted. Common ragweed and western ragweed have their own profiles.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An annual with rough stems and opposite leaves, usually with three to five deep lobes. Early leaves may be unlobed. Male flower clusters stand at the stem tips. [I-giant-ragweed](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/giant-ragweed)

## People

Windborne pollen is a significant hay-fever trigger for susceptible people. Colorado extension describes late-season ragweed exposure; local timing varies with weather. [I-giant-ragweed](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/giant-ragweed) [C-ragweed-native](https://sam.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/44/2019/08/August-31-2019-Ragweed.pdf)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

Some animals browse the foliage; that does not establish a safe feed amount or a pet-safety rating. [I-giant-ragweed](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/giant-ragweed)

*Evidence scope: Forage observation; safety limitation.*

## Plants & habitat

Early emergence and tall growth can help it dominate disturbed sites. Resistance to some herbicide groups is documented in particular populations. [I-giant-ragweed](https://crops.extension.iastate.edu/encyclopedia/giant-ragweed)

## Management & timing

Remove young plants before flowering and seed formation. For a large or persistent infestation, consult a local weed specialist about identification and resistance. [C-ragweed-native](https://sam.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/44/2019/08/August-31-2019-Ragweed.pdf)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **C-floristic-2020** — [Colorado Floristic Quality Assessment: 2020 Update](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf), Colorado Natural Heritage Program, Colorado State University. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Giant salvinia

*Salvinia molesta*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Floating mats harm aquatic habitat.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Several floating ferns look similar. Close inspection of leaf hairs is needed; no photo alone establishes identification.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A floating fern with paired surface fronds and submerged, root-like fronds. Magnification reveals characteristic joined, eggbeater-shaped hairs. [N-salvinia-molesta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/salvinia-molesta/)

## Plants & habitat

Thick mats restrict light and oxygen, clog water intakes and obstruct recreation. Do not introduce it into ponds or discard aquarium material outdoors. [N-salvinia-molesta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/salvinia-molesta/)

## People

Dense surface mats obstruct boating and swimming; this is a water-access hazard rather than a demonstrated ingestion syndrome. [N-salvinia-molesta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/salvinia-molesta/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Habitat effects include reduced oxygen and harm to fish and aquatic plants. Direct livestock toxicity is not established here. [N-salvinia-molesta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/salvinia-molesta/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Golden banner

*Thermopsis divaricarpa*

**Colorado native.** [C-perennials](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-herbaceous-perennials-for-colorado-landscapes/)

> **May poison livestock.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Do not silently substitute Thermopsis montana or T. lupinoides studies for T. divaricarpa.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Upright spikes of yellow, pea-like flowers on a native perennial. [C-perennials](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-herbaceous-perennials-for-colorado-landscapes/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads vigorously by rhizomes. Give it room in a garden; its ability to crowd smaller neighbors is a garden-management concern. [C-perennials](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-herbaceous-perennials-for-colorado-landscapes/)

## People

This review has no species-specific human exposure assessment. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

CSU Extension’s Boulder County livestock guide lists this exact species among native plants that may be poisonous to livestock. No safe dose or safe growth stage is established here. [C-banner](https://boulder.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/7/2021/03/BoCo-Rural-Living-Guide-Agriculture-Livestock-Rev-1.pdf)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **C-perennials** — [Native Herbaceous Perennials for Colorado Landscapes](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-herbaceous-perennials-for-colorado-landscapes/), Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:31:28+00:00. Locator: Other Common Plants that may be Poisonous to Livestock: Golden Banner (Thermopsis divaricarpa).

# Green foxtail

*Setaria viridis*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Identify the grass behind the name “foxtail”.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Setaria viridis is distinct from foxtail barley (Hordeum jubatum), giant foxtail (Setaria faberi) and yellow foxtail (Setaria pumila).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A summer annual grass with narrow, bristly, cylindrical seedheads. Several bristles surround each small spikelet. It commonly grows in disturbed ground and lawns. [N-setaria-viridis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/setaria-viridis/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The botanical sources describe bristly seedheads but do not establish the injury rate or ingestion toxicity of this species in pets. Avoid applying foxtail barley or cheatgrass exposure findings to every grass called foxtail. [N-setaria-viridis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/setaria-viridis/)

## Other animals

Grazing animals and small mammals eat this grass, but that observation does not provide a safe feeding amount or prove that mature seedheads are harmless. [N-setaria-viridis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/setaria-viridis/)

## Plants & habitat

It produces abundant seed and can spread rapidly on disturbed sites. Dense established vegetation is less vulnerable than open, poor-quality ground. [N-setaria-viridis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/setaria-viridis/)

## Management & timing

Repair bare lawn patches and prevent seed production. A mowed plant may still form seedheads, so inspect close to the ground. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Hairy willow-herb

*Epilobium hirsutum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Hairy stems and toothed leaves bear pink, four-petaled flowers with notched tips. Long capsules release hairy seeds. [N-epilobium-hirsutum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/epilobium-hirsutum/)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes and wind-carried seeds spread this wet-site plant. Inspect nursery stock and prevent seed dispersal. [N-epilobium-hirsutum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/epilobium-hirsutum/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

# Halogeton

*Halogeton glomeratus*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Severe oxalate poisoning in livestock.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An annual of saline disturbed ground with fleshy, narrow leaves ending in a hair-like tip; stems may redden. [A-halogeton](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/halogeton-halogeton-glomeratus/)

## Plants & habitat

Occupies disturbed dry rangeland and saline soils. Prevent seed spread and involve the local weed program. [A-halogeton](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/halogeton-halogeton-glomeratus/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Soluble oxalates can cause severe, sometimes fatal poisoning in sheep and cattle. Dry plants remain hazardous; do not assume curing or senescence makes forage safe. [A-halogeton](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/halogeton-halogeton-glomeratus/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
