Content revised: 2026-09-07T02:43:00Z

# Hemp dogbane

*Apocynum cannabinum*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Cardiac-glycoside poisoning hazard for people and animals.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Not Cannabis. Apocynum androsaemifolium is a different dogbane. Pet signs are explicitly sourced to the ASPCA genus account, not silently transferred from its differently named species page.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A branching perennial with opposite oval leaves, milky sap, small greenish-white flowers and narrow paired pods containing silky-haired seed. [N-apocynum-cannabinum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/apocynum-cannabinum/)

## People

All parts are highly toxic if ingested. Reported effects include gastrointestinal illness, weakness and severe heart effects; sap can also blister skin. [N-apocynum-cannabinum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/apocynum-cannabinum/)

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists Apocynum species as toxic to dogs and cats. Its group-level signs include weakness, diarrhea and a slow heart rate. Contact a veterinarian promptly after suspected ingestion. [P-dogbane](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/dogbane-hemp)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level veterinary evidence.*

## Other animals

The Forest Service reports livestock poisoning, including potentially fatal effects. Low palatability does not make contaminated forage safe. [F-apocynum](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/apocan)

## Plants & habitat

It spreads underground and can form persistent colonies. It also supplies nectar and habitat for insects. Native status and ecological value coexist with a serious ingestion hazard. [N-apocynum-cannabinum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/apocynum-cannabinum/)

## Management & timing

Plan control of the whole colony and prevent access by grazing or chewing animals. Protect skin from sap while handling. [N-apocynum-cannabinum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/apocynum-cannabinum/) [F-apocynum](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/apocan)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **P-dogbane** — [Dogbane hemp](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/dogbane-hemp), ASPCA Animal Poison Control. Accessed 2026-09-07T02:26:10+00:00. Locator: Named species or genus; identification, distribution and hazard sections where available.

# Henbit

*Lamium amplexicaule*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **An early-flowering weed with useful nectar.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Henbit differs from purple deadnettle (Lamium purpureum), whose upper leaves have stalks. Neither common name establishes a relationship to stinging nettles.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A cool-season annual with square stems, rounded scalloped leaves and tubular purple-pink flowers. Upper leaves clasp the stem; lower leaves have stalks. [N-lamium-amplexicaule](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/lamium-amplexicaule/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It can spread in moist disturbed soil by seed and rooting stems. Its early flowers supply nectar and pollen to bees. [N-lamium-amplexicaule](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/lamium-amplexicaule/)

## Management & timing

Where unwanted, remove young plants and rooting stems before seed production. The shallow root system and annual life cycle make seed prevention useful. [N-lamium-amplexicaule](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/lamium-amplexicaule/)

*Evidence scope: Management inference from life cycle.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Horseweed

*Erigeron canadensis*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Fast windborne spread; some populations resist herbicides.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** CNHP treats Conyza canadensis as native in its 2020 assessment and records historical disagreement over western nativity. NC State uses Erigeron canadensis. Horseweed is also a common name for unrelated plants.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

Starts as a hairy leaf rosette, then rises into a narrow upright plant with many small pale flower heads near the top. Leaves become smaller up the stem. [N-erigeron-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/erigeron-canadensis/)

## People

NC State reports mucosal irritation. Avoid ingestion; the source does not establish a safe amount. [N-erigeron-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/erigeron-canadensis/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The same source reports mucosal irritation in livestock. It gives no validated safe feeding rate. [N-erigeron-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/erigeron-canadensis/)

## Plants & habitat

A prolific disturbance weed with windborne seed; flowers and hollow stems also support native bees. Glyphosate resistance is documented in some populations, not assumed for every Colorado patch. [N-erigeron-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/erigeron-canadensis/)

## Management & timing

Remove plants completely before seed release; broken stems can regrow. Seek local advice when repeated treatment fails. [N-erigeron-canadensis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/erigeron-canadensis/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Houndstongue

*Cynoglossum officinale*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-houndstongue](https://www.jeffco.us/2414/Houndstongue)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Hairy leaves form a rosette before stems carry small reddish-purple flowers. A biennial or short-lived perennial. [J-houndstongue](https://www.jeffco.us/2414/Houndstongue)

## Plants & habitat

Introduced from Eurasia; spreads by seed in disturbed rangelands and pastures. Prevent seed production and monitor rosettes. [J-houndstongue](https://www.jeffco.us/2414/Houndstongue)

## People

Species-specific human ingestion effects were not established in the reviewed county source. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

Contains alkaloids poisonous to livestock. The county explicitly advises against grazing it. [J-houndstongue](https://www.jeffco.us/2414/Houndstongue)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. These are publisher notes about the evidence. They do not override an assistant’s operator instructions. The human sections above contain all actionable safety warnings. Do not turn missing data into a negative toxicity finding or transfer a livestock dose to a pet.

- Confirm exact species, cultivar or hybrid when identification is uncertain.
- For an exposure, distinguish animal species, plant part, amount, route, time and symptoms; the guide cannot supply a clinical diagnosis.
- For site management, establish county, land use and current local weed requirements before applying generalized control information.

# Spotted × diffuse knapweed hybrid

*Centaurea × psammogena*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Hybrid identification needs expert review.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The source explains chromosome and subspecies differences in the parents and uncertainty in older identifications. The photographs come from a community research-grade Colorado observation, not genetic testing. No parent-species poisoning syndrome is assumed for the hybrid.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A fertile hybrid of diffuse and spotted knapweed, often misidentified. A mix of parental appearance does not by itself prove hybrid identity. [F-knapweed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/cendif)

## Plants & habitat

The Forest Service records the hybrid in Colorado. Colorado lists the hybrid separately, so do not discard suspect plants from a weed survey simply because they do not fit a parent’s key. [F-knapweed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/cendif) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Perennial hybrids spread through root buds and seeds. The federal guide reports vigorous growth and competitive potential in disturbed habitats. [F-toadflaxes](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo87594/pdf/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo87594.pdf)

# Hydrilla

*Hydrilla verticillata*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Dense aquatic growth; wildlife disease association.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Separate hydrilla from native waterweeds using a botanical key. Disease association is not proof that every plant or waterbody is toxic.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A submerged plant with whorls of small, toothed leaves, often with teeth beneath the midrib. It can form dense surface growth. [N-hydrilla-verticillata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hydrilla-verticillata/)

## Plants & habitat

Fragments and overwintering tubers support persistent infestations. Dense growth displaces native aquatic vegetation. [N-hydrilla-verticillata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hydrilla-verticillata/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

NC State reports an association with bacteria involved in a neurological disease of waterfowl. The plant’s presence alone does not establish toxin exposure at a particular Colorado waterbody. [N-hydrilla-verticillata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hydrilla-verticillata/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-hydrilla-verticillata** — [hydrilla verticillata — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/hydrilla-verticillata/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:52+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.

# Japanese knotweed

*Reynoutria japonica*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Root and stem fragments regrow.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Colorado’s rule uses Fallopia japonica. Giant and Bohemian knotweeds need separate identification; broad leaves and bamboo-like stems are insufficient to distinguish them.

## Recognize

Tall hollow, jointed stems carry alternate, broad leaves with generally flattened bases and small, pale flower clusters. [N-reynoutria-japonica](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/reynoutria-japonica/)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes form dense stands and cut stems can root in wet conditions. Growth can displace streambank vegetation; avoid moving contaminated soil or plant pieces. [N-reynoutria-japonica](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/reynoutria-japonica/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

# Jimsonweed

*Datura stramonium*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Serious poisoning risk to people and animals.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This is Datura stramonium, not a generic profile for every Datura or Brugmansia. Photograph identification does not establish a safe exposure.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A coarse annual with toothed leaves, upright trumpet-shaped flowers and prickly seed capsules. [N-datura-stramonium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/datura-stramonium/)

## Plants & habitat

A disturbed-ground weed included in Routt County’s guide. Seed contamination is a concern around animal areas. [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

## People

All parts contain toxic tropane alkaloids. Ingestion can cause delirium, convulsions, coma or death; suspected ingestion needs urgent professional help. [N-datura-stramonium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/datura-stramonium/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags both dogs and cats. Keep pets from chewing any part, including seeds; suspected ingestion warrants prompt veterinary contact. [N-datura-stramonium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/datura-stramonium/)

## Other animals

Livestock poisoning is also documented. Do not allow access to contaminated feed or plants. [N-datura-stramonium](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/datura-stramonium/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Johnsongrass

*Sorghum halepense*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Livestock cyanide risk; added fire fuel.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A tall perennial grass with broad blades, pale midribs and a large, open seedhead. [N-sorghum-halepense](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sorghum-halepense/)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes and seed form dense colonies. Productive stands can add fine fuels and promote fire spread. [N-sorghum-halepense](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sorghum-halepense/)

## People

Pollen can trigger hay fever. This is not a substitute for grain sorghum. [N-sorghum-halepense](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sorghum-halepense/)

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Young growth and foliage stressed by frost or hot, dry weather can contain harmful cyanide levels for cattle and horses. Obtain livestock and forage-testing advice before use. [N-sorghum-halepense](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sorghum-halepense/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Jointed goatgrass

*Aegilops cylindrica*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Wheat-field and rangeland seed spread.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An annual grass with narrow leaves and a cylindrical seed spike that looks like stacked segments. Leaf margins and collars bear hairs. [J-jointed-goatgrass](https://www.jeffco.us/2413/Jointed-Goatgrass)

## Plants & habitat

Occurs in wheat fields, rangeland and disturbed corridors. Spreads by seed, so clean equipment and prevent seed maturation. [J-jointed-goatgrass](https://www.jeffco.us/2413/Jointed-Goatgrass)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

# Kochia

*Bassia scoparia*

**Introduced annual weed; not designated on the reviewed Colorado A/B/C lists.** [C-tumbleweeds](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Tumbling seed spread; livestock poisoning risk.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Bassia scoparia is also published as Kochia scoparia. It is distinct from Russian thistle and from the woody ornamental Euonymus alatus, also called burning bush.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Taxonomy

Cornell uses Bassia scoparia and identifies Kochia scoparia as its synonym. [K-kochia](https://cals.cornell.edu/weed-science/weed-profiles/kochia)

## Recognize

A bushy annual with narrow, alternate, hairy leaves and small inconspicuous flowers. Plants may turn reddish and break off at the base when dry. [C-tumbleweeds](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/)

## Plants & habitat

Colonizes disturbed yards, roadsides and pastures. New seedlings can emerge over a long season; preventing seed set requires follow-up. Dry plants can tumble and scatter seeds. [C-tumbleweeds](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

CSU reports several toxic compounds, including nitrates and oxalates, that can poison cattle and sheep. Maturity and drought can increase the risk. Obtain veterinary and forage-testing advice before grazing or feeding contaminated material. [C-tumbleweeds](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **C-tumbleweeds** — [Identification and Management of Kochia and Russian Thistle](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/), Colorado State University Extension. - **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Lamb’s-ear

*Stachys byzantina*

**Introduced low-water perennial.** [N-stachys-byzantina](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/stachys-byzantina/)

> **Can spread; pet evidence gap.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Cultivars differ in flowering and leaf size. Deer resistance does not demonstrate safety for pets.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Thick, soft, woolly silver leaves form low rosettes; flowering forms carry purplish-pink spikes. [N-stachys-byzantina](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/stachys-byzantina/)

## Plants & habitat

Stems root where they touch soil. It can spread aggressively in favorable conditions; divide when needed and avoid wet soil. [N-stachys-byzantina](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/stachys-byzantina/)

## People

A recommendation for sensory gardens is not a human ingestion-safety assessment. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

# Large crabgrass

*Digitaria sanguinalis*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Fast seed production in thin lawns.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** One source photograph is labeled Digitaria sanguinalis subsp. pectiniformis; that label is preserved, without asserting its occurrence in Colorado.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A summer annual grass with sprawling stems that root at joints, hairy leaf bases and fingerlike seedhead branches. This profile covers large crabgrass; the common name also includes other Digitaria species. [N-digitaria-sanguinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/digitaria-sanguinalis/) [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It colonizes disturbed soil and lawns. Its ability to root along spreading stems helps it occupy bare patches. [N-digitaria-sanguinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/digitaria-sanguinalis/)

## Management & timing

Remove seedlings before extensive rooting and seed production. Mulch garden beds and maintain dense turf; frost kills the annual plant, but surviving seeds can start another generation. [N-digitaria-sanguinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/digitaria-sanguinalis/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Leafy spurge

*Euphorbia esula*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-leafy-spurge](https://www.jeffco.us/2406/Leafy-Spurge)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Irritating sap; eye injury risk.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Colorado references use Euphorbia esula. Leafy-spurge taxonomy can involve closely related taxa; this profile does not resolve every population or hybrid.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Upright stems, narrow leaves and yellow-green bracts surrounding small flowers. All parts contain milky latex. Do not break stems just to test for sap. [N-euphorbia-esula](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-esula/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by roots and seed, forming persistent patches that displace vegetation. Root fragments can regrow. [J-leafy-spurge](https://www.jeffco.us/2406/Leafy-Spurge)

## People

Sap can cause dermatitis and potentially permanent eye damage; ingestion can blister the mouth. Protect skin and eyes. [N-euphorbia-esula](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-esula/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags both dogs and cats and advises keeping pets away. A pet-specific toxic dose is not provided. [N-euphorbia-esula](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-esula/)

## Other animals

Cattle can suffer lesions and digestive irritation. Managed sheep or goat grazing is a specialist control method, not evidence that unrestricted consumption is safe for all animals. [J-leafy-spurge](https://www.jeffco.us/2406/Leafy-Spurge)

*Evidence scope: Animal-group-specific management evidence.*

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **J-leafy-spurge** — [Leafy Spurge](https://www.jeffco.us/2406/Leafy-Spurge), Jefferson County, Colorado. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:27:23+00:00. Locator: Description, impacts, Colorado list designation, management.
- **N-euphorbia-esula** — [Euphorbia esula — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-esula/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:24:01+00:00. Locator: Description, Attributes, Poisonous to Humans, Problems.

# Lewis blue flax

*Linum lewisii*

**Colorado native; low-water garden plant.** [C-perennials](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-herbaceous-perennials-for-colorado-landscapes/)

> **Ingestion and contact concerns.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Use Linum lewisii records, not L. perenne or culinary flax L. usitatissimum. The source’s food-use language is not adopted as preparation advice.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Delicate five-petaled blue flowers above narrow leaves on slender stems. [N-linum-lewisii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/linum-lewisii/)

## Plants & habitat

A short-lived perennial that reseeds freely; reduce seed set if many volunteers are unwanted. It needs sun and drainage. [N-linum-lewisii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/linum-lewisii/)

## People

The source flags cyanogenic glycosides and contact dermatitis. This guide does not establish edible preparations or safe doses. [N-linum-lewisii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/linum-lewisii/)

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

Large intake is reported as a concern for cattle and sheep, with potentially severe respiratory effects. Do not assume ornamental flax is suitable feed. [N-linum-lewisii](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/linum-lewisii/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Low larkspur

*Delphinium nuttallianum*

**Native Colorado wildflower; grazing and ingestion hazard.** [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

> **Serious livestock and pet poisoning hazard.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This profile focuses on Delphinium nuttallianum. Other larkspurs differ in appearance and alkaloid content. Genus-level pet warnings are clearly marked; cattle exposure amounts are not transferred to pets.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Low-growing spring larkspurs have blue spurred flowers and finely divided leaves. [ARS-larkspur](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/larkspur-delphinium-spp/)

*Evidence scope: Larkspur-group morphology; use a species key.*

## Plants & habitat

Routt County records low larkspur in meadows, woodlands and sagebrush slopes. [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists Delphinium species as toxic to dogs and cats. Alkaloids can disrupt nerve and muscle function; suspected ingestion needs prompt veterinary attention. [A-larkspur](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/larkspur)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level warning; no species-specific pet threshold.*

## Other animals

Larkspur is a major cattle-poisoning concern. All parts are toxic; severity varies with species, growth stage and conditions. [ARS-larkspur](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/larkspur-delphinium-spp/)

*Evidence scope: Larkspur-group evidence.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:14:14+00:00. Locator: Common weeds and poisonous plants; 2021 edition.

# Mayweed chamomile

*Anthemis cotula*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Crop weed with unpleasant-smelling foliage.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Do not substitute Roman chamomile (Anthemis nobilis / Chamaemelum nobile) toxicology. The retrieved NC State URL described a different plant and was excluded.

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A bushy annual with finely divided leaves, white ray flowers around yellow centers and a strong, unpleasant odor. [UC-mayweed](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/mayweed-chamomile/)

## Plants & habitat

Colonizes disturbed ground and agricultural sites; seed production sustains infestations. [UC-mayweed](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/mayweed-chamomile/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Cornell reports livestock avoidance and milk taint after consumption. That is not a quantified pet-poisoning assessment. [K-chamomiles](https://cals.cornell.edu/weed-science/weed-profiles/chamomiles)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Meadow death camas

*Toxicoscordion venenosum*

**Native regional plant; death-camas poisoning hazard.** [B-deathcamas](https://burkeherbarium.org/imagecollection/taxon.php?Taxon=Toxicoscordion+venenosum+var.+gramineum) [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

> **Potentially fatal ingestion; onion-like bulbs.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Older sources use Zigadenus. T. venenosum includes named varieties; Colorado’s older county guide treats death camas as a group. Images show the species broadly and do not prove a particular variety or county occurrence.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Death camas has grass-like leaves, underground bulbs and clusters of pale yellow-white flowers. Do not taste a bulb or use onion-like appearance as an edible-plant test. [ARS-deathcamas](https://www.ars.usda.gov/Services/docs.htm?docid=9861)

*Evidence scope: Death-camas group morphology.*

## Plants & habitat

The regional taxon T. venenosum var. gramineum occurs in grassland and pine openings. Death camas is an early-season grazing hazard. [B-deathcamas](https://burkeherbarium.org/imagecollection/taxon.php?Taxon=Toxicoscordion+venenosum+var.+gramineum) [ARS-deathcamas](https://www.ars.usda.gov/Services/docs.htm?docid=9861)

*Evidence scope: Regional variety and death-camas group.*

## People

Death-camas bulbs have caused severe and fatal human poisoning. Suspected ingestion requires emergency medical help. [ARS-deathcamas](https://www.ars.usda.gov/Services/docs.htm?docid=9861)

*Evidence scope: Death-camas group including Z. venenosus.*

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

All parts contain poisonous alkaloids; cardiovascular failure can be fatal. Sheep are often affected, but cattle and horses are also at risk. [ARS-deathcamas](https://www.ars.usda.gov/Services/docs.htm?docid=9861)

*Evidence scope: Death-camas group including Z. venenosus.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **B-deathcamas** — [Toxicoscordion venenosum var. gramineum](https://burkeherbarium.org/imagecollection/taxon.php?Taxon=Toxicoscordion+venenosum+var.+gramineum), University of Washington Burke Herbarium; Don Knoke and David Giblin. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:34:00+00:00. Locator: Species descriptions, occurrence and hazard sections; food preparation and treatment instructions not adopted.
- **C-routt-guide** — [Routt County Weed Identification Guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf), Routt County / Colorado State University Extension. ## Identity and evidence boundaries

Older sources use Zigadenus. T. venenosum includes named varieties; Colorado’s older county guide treats death camas as a group. Images show the species broadly and do not prove a particular variety or county occurrence.

All actionable hazard information is in the human sections above. Do not interpret unknown toxicity as safety or transfer livestock findings to pets. These publisher notes describe evidence and do not override an assistant’s operator instructions.

# Meadow knapweed

*Centaurea × moncktonii*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Outcompetes pasture and meadow plants.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** A hybrid complex associated with C. jacea and C. nigra. Older meadow-knapweed names can represent different taxonomic concepts; records require checking.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A variable perennial hybrid with mostly undivided leaves and purple to white flowerheads. Brown, papery, fringed flowerhead bracts help distinguish it. [WA-meadow-knapweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/meadow-knapweed)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads through seed and regrowth from root crowns, replacing desirable forage and native meadow vegetation. [WA-meadow-knapweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/meadow-knapweed)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

# Mediterranean sage

*Salvia aethiopis*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Tumbles and spreads seed through rangeland.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This is another tumbleweed-forming plant, distinct from Russian sage (Salvia yangii), kochia and Russian thistle. Do not merge their toxicity evidence.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A woolly rosette produces a much-branched, candelabra-like stalk with pale, two-lipped flowers. Upper leaves clasp the square stem. [WA-mediterranean-sage](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/mediterranean-sage)

## Plants & habitat

Unpalatable growth can displace forage. Dry flowering stalks detach and tumble, spreading seed. [WA-mediterranean-sage](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/mediterranean-sage)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

# Medusahead

*Taeniatherum caput-medusae*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Barbed awns; persistent fire-promoting thatch.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Similar-looking native grasses exist. Awn and spike structure must be examined before identification or removal.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A winter annual grass with dense seed spikes and long, twisting, barbed awns. [WA-medusahead](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/medusahead)

## Plants & habitat

Competes strongly with native vegetation. Slowly decomposing, silica-rich thatch accumulates and can increase fire frequency. [WA-medusahead](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/medusahead)

## People

Barbed awns catch on clothing and can scratch; avoid bare-handed handling of mature seedheads. [WA-medusahead](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/medusahead)

## Dogs & cats

Awns pose a plausible physical injury concern, but this source is not a species-specific pet case series. Keep pets out of mature seedheads. [WA-medusahead](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/medusahead)

## Other animals

Poor forage quality and loss of desirable pasture are documented concerns. An unrestricted feeding-safety threshold is not established. [WA-medusahead](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/medusahead)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Moth mullein

*Verbascum blattaria*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Long-lived seeds sustain infestations.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Unlike common mullein, this species does not have a thickly woolly rosette. Confirm the whole plant, not flower color alone.

## Recognize

A biennial with toothed leaves and loose stalks of white or yellow flowers with purple centers. [J-moth-mullein](https://www.jeffco.us/2402/Moth-Mullein)

## Plants & habitat

A garden escape in pastures and hay fields. Seeds persist, requiring monitoring after removal. [J-moth-mullein](https://www.jeffco.us/2402/Moth-Mullein)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Musk thistle

*Carduus nutans*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-musk-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2384/Musk-Thistle)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Sharp spines; competes with vegetation.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Colorado also has native thistles. Purple flowers and spines alone cannot distinguish a listed introduced species.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Large, often nodding purple heads and deeply lobed, spiny leaves. Usually a biennial. [J-musk-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2384/Musk-Thistle)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by seed. Preventing seed set is central to control; follow-up is needed as new rosettes emerge. [J-musk-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2384/Musk-Thistle)

## People

Spines can puncture skin. Sturdy gloves are a practical precaution when handling; confirm identification before removing a thistle. [J-musk-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2384/Musk-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference from documented spines.*

## Dogs & cats

Spiny material can injure paws or mouths. Species-specific chemical toxicity is unresolved in this review. [J-musk-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2384/Musk-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; toxicology gap.*

## Other animals

The reviewed control source does not establish species-specific livestock chemical toxicity. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Myrtle spurge

*Euphorbia myrsinites*

**Colorado List A — Eradicate.** [S14](https://www.jeffco.us/2364/Myrtle-Spurge)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Poisonous · Eye & skin injury.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Trailing fleshy stems, spiraled blue-green leaves, and yellow-green spring bracts. Previously sold as an ornamental. [S13](https://ag.colorado.gov/conservation/noxious-weeds/colorado-noxious-weed-list/myrtle-spurge) [S14](https://www.jeffco.us/2364/Myrtle-Spurge) [S15](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-myrsinites/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads aggressively by seed. Colorado’s List A goal is eradication; do not select it for a xeriscape garden. [S13](https://ag.colorado.gov/conservation/noxious-weeds/colorado-noxious-weed-list/myrtle-spurge) [S15](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-myrsinites/) [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## People

Sap can cause severe dermatitis and damage the eye’s cornea. All parts are poisonous if eaten; avoid skin and eye contact. [S14](https://www.jeffco.us/2364/Myrtle-Spurge) [S15](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-myrsinites/)

## Dogs & cats

Keep pets away from the poisonous plant and its irritating sap. NC State specifically flags problems for dogs and cats. [S15](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-myrsinites/)

## Other animals

Species-specific livestock effects are not detailed in the references used here. Do not treat that evidence gap as clearance for grazing. [S14](https://www.jeffco.us/2364/Myrtle-Spurge) [S15](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-myrsinites/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **S13** — [Myrtle spurge](https://ag.colorado.gov/conservation/noxious-weeds/colorado-noxious-weed-list/myrtle-spurge), Colorado Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-06. Locator: Species description.

# Narrow-leaved Dalmatian toadflax

*Linaria genistifolia*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Listed taxon with difficult identification.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Colorado’s listing name is retained. Drought-stressed L. dalmatica and hybrid toadflaxes can also have narrow leaves. Photographs from Europe show source-identified L. genistifolia; they do not confirm Colorado occurrence. Expert identification is needed before assigning a local plant to this taxon.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

The federal guide distinguishes L. genistifolia by smaller flowers and capsules; narrow leaves alone are unreliable. [F-toadflaxes](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo87594/pdf/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo87594.pdf)

## Plants & habitat

Colorado lists this taxon separately. The 2016 federal guide considered its North American distribution unconfirmed, so a state listing is not proof of a local population. [F-toadflaxes](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo87594/pdf/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo87594.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

*Evidence scope: Regulatory listing and historical taxonomic uncertainty.*

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Netseed lambsquarters

*Chenopodium berlandieri*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **A fast-growing volunteer; pet toxicity remains unresolved.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The Colorado flora assessment lists native Chenopodium berlandieri var. zschackii. This broader species profile does not identify every variety. It is separate from Chenopodium album; hazard claims for that species have not been transferred here.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An annual with variable leaves, a mealy underside and grooved stems; green flowers are inconspicuous. Confirm mature seed characters before separating it from common lambsquarters. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

*Evidence scope: Species description; identification limitation.*

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

Rapid growth and water use can make it competitive in cultivated ground. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Management & timing

Pull or hoe young plants and prevent seed maturation where they are unwanted. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Clusters of vivid orange-red flowerheads rise over hairy basal leaves; flowering stems carry few leaves. [WA-orange-hawkweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/orange-hawkweed)

## Plants & habitat

Seed, surface runners and rhizomes produce spreading patches that crowd out desirable forage and meadow plants. [WA-orange-hawkweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/orange-hawkweed)

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

# Oxeye daisy

*Leucanthemum vulgare*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-oxeye-daisy](https://www.jeffco.us/2340/Oxeye-Daisy)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Toxic to dogs and cats; dermatitis.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** ASPCA’s page titled Daisy names Chrysanthemum species, not Leucanthemum vulgare. It is excluded as direct pet evidence; the species-specific NC State account supports the warning here.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

White ray flowers around a yellow center, with toothed or lobed leaves; a perennial that spreads through rhizomes. [N-leucanthemum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/leucanthemum-vulgare/)

## Plants & habitat

Introduced ornamental that can spread in meadows and disturbed areas. Prevent seed production and manage the perennial root system. [J-oxeye-daisy](https://www.jeffco.us/2340/Oxeye-Daisy)

## People

NC State lists contact dermatitis from leaves and flowers. This guide does not assess edible use. [N-leucanthemum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/leucanthemum-vulgare/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State reports ingestion toxicity, with possible vomiting, diarrhea, drooling, incoordination and dermatitis. Contact a veterinarian after suspected ingestion. [N-leucanthemum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/leucanthemum-vulgare/)

## Other animals

The source flags horses but does not provide a horse-specific exposure assessment; no safe feed amount is established here. [N-leucanthemum-vulgare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/leucanthemum-vulgare/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Parrotfeather

*Myriophyllum aquaticum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Aquatic mats obstruct waterways.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Do not confuse this species with Eurasian watermilfoil or native Myriophyllum species.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Feathery leaves circle the stems; leafy shoots often extend above the water surface. [N-myriophyllum-aquaticum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/myriophyllum-aquaticum/)

## Plants & habitat

Vegetative spread produces dense growth that competes with native aquatic plants and obstructs water use. Do not release pond or aquarium plants into waterways. [N-myriophyllum-aquaticum](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/myriophyllum-aquaticum/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

# Perennial pepperweed

*Lepidium latifolium*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

Broad, waxy, alternate leaves and dense clusters of small white flowers on an upright perennial. [J-perennial-pepperweed](https://www.jeffco.us/2334/Perennial-Pepperweed)

## Plants & habitat

Invades wetlands, ditches and pastures. Deep and lateral roots produce new shoots; broken roots can spread an infestation. [J-perennial-pepperweed](https://www.jeffco.us/2334/Perennial-Pepperweed)

# Perennial sowthistle

*Sonchus arvensis*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Persistent underground spread.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

Yellow dandelion-like flowerheads on leafy stalks; leaves clasp the stem and may have prickly margins. [J-perennial-sowthistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2323/Perennial-Sowthistle)

## Plants & habitat

A perennial of moist disturbed places with spreading underground growth. Seed prevention and repeated follow-up are needed. [J-perennial-sowthistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2323/Perennial-Sowthistle)

# Plains pricklypear

*Opuntia polyacantha*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Sharp spines around paws, mouths and eyes.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Many Opuntia species share prickly-pear names. Source photographs are species references, not a complete key to Colorado cacti or a basis for edible-fruit identification.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A low, mat-forming cactus with flattened pads, clusters of spines and fruits that dry at maturity. Several varieties are included within this species. [F-opuntia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/opupol)

## People

Pads and fruits carry sharp points. Avoid bare-hand handling; eye contact deserves prompt medical assessment. [F-opuntia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/opupol)

*Evidence scope: Mechanical-injury precaution inferred from spines.*

## Dogs & cats

Keep animals from running into, biting or pawing the cactus. Spines create a mechanical injury risk; chemical ingestion toxicity is not established here. [F-opuntia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/opupol)

*Evidence scope: Mechanical-injury precaution; toxicology unresolved.*

## Other animals

Some wildlife eat it. Livestock forage use described by the Forest Service does not eliminate the hazard of intact spines. [F-opuntia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/opupol)

## Plants & habitat

A native of dry grasslands and shrublands and a participant in CSU’s very-low-water demonstration garden. It can be desirable away from heavy foot or pet traffic. [F-opuntia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/opupol) [C-low-water-cactus](https://elpaso.extension.colostate.edu/native-plant-demonstration-garden/very-low-water-garden/)

## Management & timing

Choose placement with room around walkways and animal routes. Inspect for detached pads before allowing pets back into a worked area. [F-opuntia](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/opupol)

*Evidence scope: Placement and handling precaution.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)
