# Plumeless thistle

*Carduus acanthoides*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Sharp spines; pasture competition.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Distinguish introduced Carduus from Colorado’s native thistles before removal.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A spiny thistle with winged stems and relatively small, reddish-purple flowerheads. [J-plumeless-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2294/Plumeless-Thistle)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by seed through disturbed pasture and roadsides. Follow-up is needed as rosettes germinate. [J-plumeless-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2294/Plumeless-Thistle)

## People

Spines on leaves, stems and flowerhead bracts can puncture skin; use protective handling. [J-plumeless-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2294/Plumeless-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Spines may injure mouths or paws. Pet-specific chemical toxicity is not established here. [J-plumeless-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2294/Plumeless-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; ingestion evidence gap.*

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Poison hemlock

*Conium maculatum*

**Colorado List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [S16](https://www.jeffco.us/2230/Poison-Hemlock)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Poisonous to people & animals.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Finely divided leaves, stems with purple spots, and white umbrella-like flower clusters. Often near streams, ditches, and pasture edges. [S16](https://www.jeffco.us/2230/Poison-Hemlock)

## Plants & habitat

An introduced, colony-forming biennial. Garden judgment: exclude access and seek local management help once identification is confirmed. [S16](https://www.jeffco.us/2230/Poison-Hemlock)

## People

All parts are poisonous. Do not eat or taste it to identify it. Suspected ingestion warrants immediate Poison Control advice. [S16](https://www.jeffco.us/2230/Poison-Hemlock) [S26](https://www.poison.org/first-aid-for-poisonings)

## Dogs & cats

The county warning covers animals broadly. Treat suspected ingestion by a pet as urgent and contact a veterinarian. [S16](https://www.jeffco.us/2230/Poison-Hemlock) [S04](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/milkweed)

## Other animals

Poisonous to livestock; do not use grazing as a control method. A List C designation does not indicate low toxicity. [S16](https://www.jeffco.us/2230/Poison-Hemlock)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **S16** — [Poison Hemlock](https://www.jeffco.us/2230/Poison-Hemlock), Jefferson County, Colorado. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:54+00:00. Locator: List C designation; General; Habitat; Vegetation; Flower.
- **S26** — [First aid guidelines](https://www.poison.org/first-aid-for-poisonings), National Capital Poison Center. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:56+00:00. Locator: Poison center phone; emergency symptoms; do not induce vomiting.
- **S04** — [Milkweed](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/milkweed), ASPCA Poison Control. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:52+00:00. Locator: Scientific Name; Toxicity; Clinical Signs; poison contact.

# Prairie sage

*Artemisia ludoviciana*

**Colorado native.** [S03](https://www.extension.colostate.edu/docs/pubs/native/FrontRangeSm.pdf)

> **Spreading roots; pet evidence gap.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Common name white sage also refers to other plants, including Salvia apiana. Do not merge their uses or toxicology.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An upright silver-leaved perennial with small, inconspicuous flowerheads. [N-artemisia-ludoviciana](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/artemisia-ludoviciana/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by rhizomes and seed, sometimes becoming weedy. Dry, well-drained soil suits it; leave room or manage expansion at bed edges. [N-artemisia-ludoviciana](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/artemisia-ludoviciana/)

## People

The reviewed gardening source does not establish human ingestion safety. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

The Front Range guide records pollen and seed value for insects and birds. Those benefits do not establish livestock or pet safety. [S03](https://www.extension.colostate.edu/docs/pubs/native/FrontRangeSm.pdf)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Prickly lettuce

*Lactuca serriola*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Prickly leaves and prolific windborne seed.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Wild lettuce names refer to several species. Lactuca serriola is not a substitute for cultivated lettuce or a verified medicinal product.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An upright annual or biennial with milky sap, spines along the underside of the leaf midrib and small yellow flower heads. Leaves may stand nearly vertical. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## People

Leaf spines are a handling concern. No ingestion safety is established by this profile. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

*Evidence scope: Handling precaution inferred from leaf structure.*

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It invades disturbed garden soils. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Management & timing

Pull young plants from moist soil before flowers mature. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Prickly sowthistle

*Sonchus asper*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Spiny leaves and rapid seed production.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This is an annual Sonchus, not a true Cirsium thistle and not perennial sowthistle. Confirm leaf bases and life cycle.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An annual with yellow flower heads and often glossy, prickly-edged leaves. Rounded basal leaf lobes clasp the stem, unlike the more pointed lobes of annual sowthistle. [N-sonchus-asper](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sonchus-asper/) [U-sowthistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/annual-sowthistle/)

## People

Spiny leaf edges warrant care when handling. No ingestion safety has been established here. [N-sonchus-asper](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sonchus-asper/)

*Evidence scope: Handling precaution inferred from leaf structure.*

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It grows in gardens, roadsides and disturbed ground. Plants can bolt quickly, and seeds travel by wind and water. [N-sonchus-asper](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sonchus-asper/)

## Management & timing

Inspect patches often and remove unwanted plants before fluffy seedheads form. [N-sonchus-asper](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/sonchus-asper/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Prostrate knotweed

*Polygonum aviculare*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **A mat-forming weed of compacted ground.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This small lawn weed is not Japanese knotweed or another large invasive Reynoutria species. Polygonum aviculare is treated broadly here. The flower photo is source-labeled Polygonum aviculare subsp. depressum (s. lat.). Segregate-taxon boundaries remain under research; no subspecies determination is made for Colorado specimens.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

Look for wiry, low stems, alternating small leaves and papery sheaths around the swollen joints. Tiny flowers appear where the leaves meet the stems. [N-polygonum-aviculare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/polygonum-aviculare/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It tolerates trampling, poor soil and pavement cracks. A persistent seed supply helps it return where desirable vegetation struggles. [N-polygonum-aviculare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/polygonum-aviculare/)

## Management & timing

Mowing misses much of this low plant. Address compaction in affected turf and remove small plants before seed production. [N-polygonum-aviculare](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/polygonum-aviculare/) [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Puncturevine / goathead

*Tribulus terrestris*

**Colorado List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [S29](https://www.larimer.gov/sites/default/files/uploads/2023/weed_management_reference_guide_-_5.5_x_8_-_jan_24_2023_-_website_reader_spreads_0.pdf)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Sharp burs · Animal injury.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Low, radiating stems with small leaflets, yellow flowers, and hard, spiny burs. Common on disturbed ground. [S17](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2109-puncture-vine/)

## Plants & habitat

Can form spreading mats and compete with other plants. Preventing seed production is central to management. [S18](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/home-and-landscape/puncturevine/)

## People

Spiny burs puncture skin and can remain a hazard after the plant dies. Use protective gloves when handling. [S17](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2109-puncture-vine/) [S18](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/home-and-landscape/puncturevine/)

## Dogs & cats

Burs can injure animals. Pet precaution: keep play areas clear of burs and check paws after contact. [S18](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/home-and-landscape/puncturevine/) [S19](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/here-they-come/)

## Other animals

Burs can injure grazing animals’ mouths. Puncturevine can also be toxic to sheep; livestock findings do not establish a pet toxicity dose. [S18](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/home-and-landscape/puncturevine/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **S29** — [Weed Management Reference Guide](https://www.larimer.gov/sites/default/files/uploads/2023/weed_management_reference_guide_-_5.5_x_8_-_jan_24_2023_-_website_reader_spreads_0.pdf), Larimer County, Colorado. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:24:00+00:00. Locator: Puncturevine entry: List C.
- **S17** — [2109 – Puncture Vine](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2109-puncture-vine/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:56+00:00. Locator: What is a puncture vine?; How can I control puncture vines?.
- **S18** — [Puncturevine / Home and Landscape](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/home-and-landscape/puncturevine/), University of California Statewide IPM. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:55+00:00. Locator: Pest Notes introduction; Identification and Life Cycle; Impact; Management.
- **S19** — [Here They Come!](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/here-they-come/), CSU Extension · Pueblo County. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:54+00:00. Locator: Puncturevine seed-casings and animal injury; herbicide label guidance.

# Purple loosestrife

*Lythrum salicaria*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Crowds out wetland vegetation.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Upright spikes of purple-pink flowers above lance-shaped, opposite or whorled leaves. Stems are angular. [N-lythrum-salicaria](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/lythrum-salicaria/)

## Plants & habitat

Extensive roots and abundant seeds enable wetland invasion. Cultivar labels do not reliably establish an inability to spread. [N-lythrum-salicaria](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/lythrum-salicaria/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Replacement of native wetland plants reduces vegetation important to waterfowl. This ecological harm is separate from direct animal poisoning. [N-lythrum-salicaria](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/lythrum-salicaria/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-lythrum-salicaria** — [lythrum salicaria — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/lythrum-salicaria/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:52+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.

# Quackgrass

*Elymus repens*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Rhizome fragments regrow in lawns and gardens.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A perennial grass with clasping auricles at the leaf collar and pale underground rhizomes with sharp tips. [M-quackgrass](https://extension.umn.edu/garden-and-home/yard-and-garden/lawns-and-landscapes-in-minnesota/quackgrass)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes compete with desired vegetation and regrow from pieces. Repeated disturbance without removing or exhausting them can spread the patch. [M-quackgrass](https://extension.umn.edu/garden-and-home/yard-and-garden/lawns-and-landscapes-in-minnesota/quackgrass)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Red valerian

*Centranthus ruber*

**Introduced drought-tolerant ornamental.** [N-centranthus-ruber](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centranthus-ruber/)

> **Self-seeds beyond garden beds.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The retrieved NC State source now treats Centranthus ruber as Valeriana rubra. The familiar garden name is retained for discovery; it is not Valeriana officinalis.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Clusters of small red, pink or white flowers above opposite, fleshy leaves. [N-centranthus-ruber](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centranthus-ruber/)

## Plants & habitat

Wind-dispersed seed can naturalize beyond a garden. Deadheading reduces spread; drought tolerance is not evidence that a plant is ecologically suitable everywhere. [N-centranthus-ruber](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centranthus-ruber/)

## People

This guide does not assess ingestion safety or medicinal use. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

# Redroot pigweed

*Amaranthus retroflexus*

**Common weed; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed state rule.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Crop competition; nitrate and oxalate livestock risk.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Pigweed is used for several unrelated or similar-looking plants. Routt County’s prostrate pigweed entry concerns A. blitoides and is not used as exact A. retroflexus evidence.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An upright annual with hairy stems and dense green flower clusters; the lower stem or root may be reddish. [N-amaranthus-retroflexus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/amaranthus-retroflexus/)

## Plants & habitat

A Colorado garden and field weed that competes strongly with crops and replenishes a seedbank. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/) [N-amaranthus-retroflexus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/amaranthus-retroflexus/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Nitrate and oxalate accumulation can poison livestock. Conditions affect risk, so a plant’s appearance does not establish forage safety. [N-amaranthus-retroflexus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/amaranthus-retroflexus/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Rubber rabbitbrush

*Ericameria nauseosa*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **A native low-water shrub that can spread readily.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Ericameria nauseosa was formerly Chrysothamnus nauseosus. A manufacturer entry for Chrysothamnus spp. is only partly represented by this profile; other rabbitbrush species remain separate research candidates.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A rounded shrub with narrow aromatic leaves, green to silvery stems and clusters of yellow flowers late in the season. Size and appearance vary among varieties. [C-native-shrubs](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-shrubs-for-colorado-landscapes/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

Wildlife browse it, especially seasonally. An older Forest Service synthesis also reports slight livestock toxicity; it does not provide pet-specific evidence or a validated safe ration. [F-ericameria](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/erinau)

*Evidence scope: Older range synthesis; clinical details limited.*

## Plants & habitat

CSU recommends it for native landscapes and notes that it can be aggressive. Late flowers attract butterflies; unwanted seedlings are a local garden-management issue. [C-native-shrubs](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-shrubs-for-colorado-landscapes/)

## Management & timing

Allow space for the mature shrub and watch for seedlings. Assess established native stands in their habitat context before removal. [C-native-shrubs](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/native-shrubs-for-colorado-landscapes/) [F-ericameria](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/erinau)

*Evidence scope: Landscape management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Rush skeletonweed

*Chondrilla juncea*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Deep roots and fragments sustain spread.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A basal rosette gives way to nearly leafless, branching stems with small yellow flowerheads. Lower stems have coarse, downward-pointing hairs. [WA-rush-skeletonweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/rush-skeletonweed)

## Plants & habitat

Wind-carried seeds and root buds spread the plant. Deep roots and fragments can regenerate; infestations displace forage and native vegetation. [WA-rush-skeletonweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/rush-skeletonweed)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **WA-rush-skeletonweed** — [Rush Skeletonweed](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/rush-skeletonweed), Washington State Noxious Weed Control Board. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:09:01+00:00. Locator: Identification, ecological impacts, management; Washington legal class not applied to Colorado.

# Russian knapweed

*Rhaponticum repens*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-russian-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/4107/Russian-knapweed)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Toxic to horses.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The county page misspells the old genus as Arcoptilon; Acroptilon repens is the synonym used by NC State.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Pink to lavender heads with tan, papery bracts; creeping dark roots beneath a perennial stand. [J-russian-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/4107/Russian-knapweed)

## Plants & habitat

Spreading roots produce persistent colonies that displace native vegetation. Cutting alone does not resolve the deep root system. [N-rhaponticum-repens](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rhaponticum-repens/) [J-russian-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/4107/Russian-knapweed)

## People

Human ingestion effects were not established in this review. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

NC State tags the plant non-toxic to dogs and cats but provides no supporting pet assessment in its main text. We retain that limited claim without treating it as a safety clearance. [N-rhaponticum-repens](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rhaponticum-repens/)

## Other animals

Poisonous to horses. Keep it out of horse pasture and hay and contact a veterinarian after suspected ingestion. [N-rhaponticum-repens](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rhaponticum-repens/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **J-russian-knapweed** — [Russian knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/4107/Russian-knapweed), Jefferson County, Colorado. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:27:24+00:00. Locator: Description, impacts, Colorado list designation, management.
- **N-rhaponticum-repens** — [Rhaponticum repens — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/rhaponticum-repens/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:24:01+00:00. Locator: Description, Attributes, Poisonous to Humans, Problems.

# Russian olive

*Elaeagnus angustifolia*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-russian-olive](https://www.jeffco.us/2224/Russian-Olive)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Thorns; riparian habitat concern.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A woody shrub or tree with narrow, silvery leaves, fragrant yellow flowers and olive-shaped fruits. Branches may bear long thorns. [J-russian-olive](https://www.jeffco.us/2224/Russian-Olive)

## Plants & habitat

An introduced plant of riparian areas and old windbreaks. Stumps, roots and discarded twigs can regrow; plan removal and follow-up together. [J-russian-olive](https://www.jeffco.us/2224/Russian-Olive)

## People

Thorns can puncture skin and eyes. Protective handling is a physical-risk precaution; this profile does not assess fruit consumption. [J-russian-olive](https://www.jeffco.us/2224/Russian-Olive)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Thorny branches can injure animals. Pet-specific chemical toxicity is not established in this review. [J-russian-olive](https://www.jeffco.us/2224/Russian-Olive)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; toxicology gap.*

## Other animals

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Russian sage

*Salvia yangii*

**Introduced low-water garden perennial.** [N-russian-sage](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/salvia-yangii/) [C-russian-sage](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/annuals-perennials/1050-russian-sage/)

> **Spreads by suckers; pet evidence unresolved.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** NC State uses Salvia yangii; older Colorado guidance uses Perovskia atriplicifolia. The older statement that this plant is outside Salvia is outdated. Culinary sage (Salvia officinalis) safety claims do not apply automatically.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A woody-based perennial with silvery, aromatic, finely divided leaves and airy purple flower spikes. The stems are square. [N-russian-sage](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/salvia-yangii/)

## Plants & habitat

An introduced plant suited to sunny, well-drained Colorado gardens. Shoots can spread beyond the original clump by suckers; remove unwanted shoots and allow room at bed edges. [C-russian-sage](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/annuals-perennials/1050-russian-sage/) [E-sage-suckers](https://ask.extension.org/kb/faq.php?id=639127)

*Evidence scope: Horticultural evidence; county extension advice.*

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Flowers attract pollinators. Reported deer and rabbit resistance is not evidence of livestock or pet feeding safety. [N-russian-sage](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/salvia-yangii/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Russian thistle

*Salsola tragus*

**Introduced annual weed; not designated on the reviewed Colorado A/B/C lists.** [C-tumbleweeds](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Prickly tumbleweeds; livestock and fire hazards.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Russian thistle is a common name for several similar Salsola taxa. This profile uses S. tragus as its principal name; UC’s identification and dry-plant hazards address a species complex. A round tumbleweed alone does not establish the species.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A branching annual that becomes rounded and prickly. Mature leaves and bracts have stiff tips; stems often show reddish stripes. Dry plants can detach and tumble. [UC-russian-thistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/russian-thistle/)

*Evidence scope: Russian-thistle species complex.*

## Plants & habitat

Invades disturbed ground. Dry accumulations can obstruct traffic and create a fire hazard; prevent seed production before plants detach. [UC-russian-thistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/russian-thistle/) [C-tumbleweeds](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/)

*Evidence scope: Species-group hazards; editorial prevention priority.*

## People

Stiff, sharp parts can puncture skin. Wear sturdy gloves when handling dry plants; this is a physical-risk inference, not a human poison diagnosis. [UC-russian-thistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/russian-thistle/)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Prickly material may injure paws or mouths. The reviewed livestock findings do not establish a pet toxic dose. Keep pets away from dry piles. [UC-russian-thistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/russian-thistle/)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; pet toxicology gap.*

## Other animals

CSU reports nitrate and oxalate poisoning risks for cattle and sheep, including severe breathing problems or kidney damage. Do not assume this weed is suitable forage. [C-tumbleweeds](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **C-tumbleweeds** — [Identification and Management of Kochia and Russian Thistle](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/identification-and-management-of-kochia-and-russian-thistle/), Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:57:25+00:00. Locator: Weed Description; Livestock Poisonings; Integrated Weed Management.
- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **UC-russian-thistle** — [Russian Thistle](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/russian-thistle/), University of California Statewide IPM. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:57:25+00:00. Locator: Taxonomic complex, morphology, oxalates, dry tumbleweed hazards.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Scentless chamomile

*Tripleurospermum inodorum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

## Recognize

White ray flowers surround a yellow center above finely divided, nearly odorless leaves. [J-scentless-chamomile](https://www.jeffco.us/2205/Scentless-Chamomile)

## Plants & habitat

Dense seedlings develop in meadows and disturbed ground. Many persistent seeds and regrowth after mowing complicate control. [J-scentless-chamomile](https://www.jeffco.us/2205/Scentless-Chamomile)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The county reports possible blistering in livestock but provides limited clinical detail. Do not assume unrestricted grazing is safe. [J-scentless-chamomile](https://www.jeffco.us/2205/Scentless-Chamomile)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Scotch thistle

*Onopordum acanthium*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-scotch-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2192/Scotch-Thistle)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

A large, woolly biennial with broad leaves, purple heads and conspicuous spiny stem wings. [J-scotch-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2192/Scotch-Thistle)

## Plants & habitat

Can form dense rosettes and persist through a long-lived seed bank. Maintain competing vegetation and prevent seed production. [J-scotch-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2192/Scotch-Thistle)

## People

Spines can puncture skin. Sturdy gloves are a practical precaution when handling; confirm identification before removing a thistle. [J-scotch-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2192/Scotch-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference from documented spines.*

## Dogs & cats

Spiny material can injure paws or mouths. Species-specific chemical toxicity is unresolved in this review. [J-scotch-thistle](https://www.jeffco.us/2192/Scotch-Thistle)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference; toxicology gap.*

## Other animals

The reviewed control source does not establish species-specific livestock chemical toxicity. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. # Shepherd’s purse

*Capsella bursa-pastoris*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **An early-seeding lawn and garden weed.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A small annual mustard with a basal leaf rosette, tiny white flowers and flat, heart-shaped or triangular seedpods. Seedlings can establish in spring or autumn. [N-capsella-bursa-pastoris](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/capsella-bursa-pastoris/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It readily colonizes disturbed ground and reseeds in gardens and roadsides. Drought and compaction tolerance help it persist. [N-capsella-bursa-pastoris](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/capsella-bursa-pastoris/)

## Management & timing

Pull or hoe before the distinctive pods mature. Check again after another flush of seedlings. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Showy milkweed

*Asclepias speciosa*

**Colorado native.** [S02](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/low-water-native-plants-for-pollinators/) [S28](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/loving-our-native-plants/)

> **Toxic if eaten.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Pink flower clusters on an upright perennial; included in the Front Range native plant guide. [S03](https://www.extension.colostate.edu/docs/pubs/native/FrontRangeSm.pdf)

## Plants & habitat

Self-seeds and supports monarch caterpillars. Garden judgment: give volunteers room or manage seedlings beside smaller plants. [S02](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/low-water-native-plants-for-pollinators/)

## People

Milkweed ingestion can cause serious poisoning; sap may irritate skin and eyes. Wear gloves when handling. [S05](https://www.poison.org/articles/milkweed-can-cause-serious-poisoning-204)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level.*

## Dogs & cats

Milkweeds are toxic to both. Signs can include vomiting and weakness, with potentially severe effects. Contact a veterinarian promptly after suspected ingestion. [S04](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/milkweed)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level.*

## Other animals

Milkweeds can poison horses and other livestock, while monarch caterpillars use this species as food. [S04](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/milkweed) [S02](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/low-water-native-plants-for-pollinators/)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level toxicity; species-specific benefit.*

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **S02** — [Low Water Native Plants for Pollinators](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/low-water-native-plants-for-pollinators/), CSU Extension · Pueblo County. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:52+00:00. Locator: Mid-Season Wildflowers: Showy Milkweed.
- **S28** — [Loving Our Native Plants](https://pueblo.extension.colostate.edu/loving-our-native-plants/), CSU Extension · Pueblo County. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:56+00:00. Locator: Native landscape plant list: Common sunflower and Showy milkweed.
- **S03** — [Low-Water Native Plants for Colorado Gardens: Front Range & Foothills](https://www.extension.colostate.edu/docs/pubs/native/FrontRangeSm.pdf), Colorado Native Plant Society / CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:53+00:00. Locator: Plant table: Asclepias speciosa and Achillea millefolium.
- **S05** — [Milkweed plants can cause serious poisoning](https://www.poison.org/articles/milkweed-can-cause-serious-poisoning-204), National Capital Poison Center. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:53+00:00. Locator: Symptoms; Effects on skin and in eyes; prevention.

# Siberian elm

*Ulmus pumila*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Numerous seedlings; weak branches.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Weak-wood descriptions support the structural concern; this guide cannot assess the stability of an individual tree.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A deciduous tree with small, toothed leaves and round, papery winged fruits in spring. [N-ulmus-pumila](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ulmus-pumila/)

## Plants & habitat

Abundant seeds create colonies in disturbed ground and grasslands; small seedlings are easier to manage than established trees. [N-ulmus-pumila](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ulmus-pumila/)

## People

Weak wood and branches are a structural concern. Have a qualified arborist assess large trees near occupied areas. [N-ulmus-pumila](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ulmus-pumila/)

## Dogs & cats

Falling limbs can endanger animals as well as people; this is a structural-risk inference. Pet ingestion effects are unresolved. [N-ulmus-pumila](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ulmus-pumila/)

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Silverleaf nightshade

*Solanum elaeagnifolium*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Toxic leaves and berries; persistent spreading roots.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Distinct from buffalobur, horsenettle and black nightshade species. Native status does not imply harmlessness or prevent it being invasive outside its native range.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A perennial with silvery, hairy leaves, often prickly stems and showy violet flowers. Young root sprouts may look like seedlings but lack seed leaves. [U-silverleaf](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/silverleaf-nightshade/)

## People

Leaves and berries contain variable amounts of toxic glycoalkaloids. Do not eat this plant or use berry color as a safety test. [U-silverleaf](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/silverleaf-nightshade/)

## Dogs & cats

Keep pets from chewing this identified nightshade as a precaution. The reviewed source documents human and livestock toxicity, but does not establish pet-specific clinical effects or a safe amount. [U-silverleaf](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/silverleaf-nightshade/)

*Evidence scope: Precautionary inference; pet evidence unresolved.*

## Other animals

Leaves and berries can poison livestock. Consumption by wild birds or small mammals does not establish safety for domestic animals. [U-silverleaf](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/silverleaf-nightshade/)

## Plants & habitat

Deep roots and persistent growth can reduce pasture or crop production through competition for water and nutrients. CNHP treats the species as native in Colorado. [U-silverleaf](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/silverleaf-nightshade/) [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf)

## Management & timing

Distinguish seedlings from root sprouts and plan repeated monitoring of established patches. Seek local management advice for extensive infestations. [U-silverleaf](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/silverleaf-nightshade/)

*Evidence scope: Management inference from root persistence.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **C-floristic-2020** — [Colorado Floristic Quality Assessment: 2020 Update](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf), Colorado Natural Heritage Program, Colorado State University. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **U-silverleaf** — [Silverleaf nightshade](https://ipm.ucanr.edu/weeds-identification-gallery/silverleaf-nightshade/), University of California Statewide Integrated Pest Management. Accessed 2026-09-07T02:26:10+00:00. Locator: Named species or genus; identification, distribution and hazard sections where available.

# Snow-in-summer

*Cerastium tomentosum*

**Introduced garden groundcover.** [S12](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cerastium-tomentosum/) [S11](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/ground-cover-plants/)

> **Spreading habit · Safety gap.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A low mat of woolly, pale foliage and white flowers. Originates in Italy; CSU lists it for hot, dry, sunny groundcover sites. [S12](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cerastium-tomentosum/) [S11](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/ground-cover-plants/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by underground runners and seed. CSU describes it as aggressive; garden judgment: keep it from overtaking smaller neighbors. [S11](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/ground-cover-plants/) [S12](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cerastium-tomentosum/)

## People

The sources used for this profile do not establish human ingestion safety. [S11](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/ground-cover-plants/) [S12](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cerastium-tomentosum/)

## Dogs & cats

No species-specific veterinary safety conclusion was established in this review. Do not interpret that gap as pet-safe. [S11](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/ground-cover-plants/) [S12](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cerastium-tomentosum/) [S25](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants)

## Other animals

Flowers attract bees. Safety for grazing animals is not established by the sources reviewed. [S12](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cerastium-tomentosum/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Snow-on-the-mountain

*Euphorbia marginata*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Latex can damage skin and eyes; toxic if eaten.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The same common name is used for Aegopodium podagraria, an unrelated groundcover. This profile and its sap warnings apply to Euphorbia marginata.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An upright annual whose upper leaves develop striking white margins. Tiny flowers sit within the showy pale bracts. Damaged tissues exude milky latex. [N-euphorbia-marginata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-marginata/)

## People

Sap can cause dermatitis, mouth blistering and serious eye damage. Wear gloves and prevent sap contact, especially with eyes. [N-euphorbia-marginata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-marginata/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags this species as hazardous to pets. Prevent chewing and contact with latex; the source provides no safe pet exposure amount. [N-euphorbia-marginata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-marginata/)

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

A native plains annual also grown as an ornamental. It tolerates dry, poor soil and can reseed into unwanted patches. [N-euphorbia-marginata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-marginata/)

## Management & timing

Handle with protection and remove unwanted plants before seed maturation. Keep freshly cut material inaccessible to children and animals. [N-euphorbia-marginata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-marginata/)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Soapweed yucca

*Yucca glauca*

**Great Plains native.** [S09](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/yucca-glauca/)

> **Sharp leaves · Toxic to pets.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A rosette of narrow, pointed leaves with upright stalks of pale, downward-facing flowers. Suited to dry, rocky soil. [S09](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/yucca-glauca/)

## Plants & habitat

Can expand through offsets and rhizomes. Garden judgment: allow room for the colony and sharp foliage. [S09](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/yucca-glauca/)

## People

Needle-sharp leaf tips are a physical hazard. Human ingestion safety is not assessed here. [S09](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/yucca-glauca/)

*Evidence scope: Species-specific physical feature; risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists Yucca species as toxic; vomiting is a reported sign. The evidence is for the genus, not an individual cultivar. [S10](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/yucca)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level.*

## Other animals

ASPCA reports liver disease and dermatitis in horses exposed to yucca. Yucca glauca also supports yucca moths and wildlife habitat. [S10](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/yucca) [S09](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/yucca-glauca/)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level toxicity; species-specific benefit.*

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Spotted knapweed

*Centaurea stoebe*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-spotted-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/2181/Spotted-Knapweed)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Competes with native vegetation.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Some Colorado references use Centaurea maculosa for spotted knapweed. We retain their name in the citation record and use C. stoebe as the catalog label.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Purple flowerheads with dark-tipped, fringed bracts; a short-lived perennial spreading mainly by seed. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Plants & habitat

An introduced noxious weed. Preventing seed production and maintaining desirable vegetation are parts of long-term control. [J-spotted-knapweed](https://www.jeffco.us/2181/Spotted-Knapweed)

## People

This review did not establish a species-specific human toxicity profile. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

Do not transfer Russian-knapweed horse toxicity to spotted knapweed without species-specific evidence. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Spotted spurge

*Euphorbia maculata*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Milky sap can injure skin and eyes; keep pets from chewing.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** CNHP treats Chamaesyce maculata as native, while documenting disagreement among earlier floras. NC State places it in Euphorbia. The common name prostrate spurge also applies to Euphorbia prostrata.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A low annual mat with opposite, often purple-spotted leaves and hairy stems. Very small plants can already flower and set seed. Injured stems release white latex; do not break plants with bare hands to test this. [N-euphorbia-maculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-maculata/)

## People

Latex can cause dermatitis and eye injury; ingestion can irritate or blister the mouth. Wear gloves and avoid sap contact, especially with eyes. [N-euphorbia-maculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-maculata/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State identifies this species as a pet hazard. Keep animals from chewing or contacting freshly damaged plants; a safe exposure amount is not established. [N-euphorbia-maculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-maculata/)

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

It rapidly reseeds disturbed beds, cracks and thin lawns. Native status does not prevent it becoming troublesome in a garden. [N-euphorbia-maculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-maculata/)

## Management & timing

Remove plants early with gloves. Low growth escapes mowing, and even small seedlings may produce seed. [N-euphorbia-maculata](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/euphorbia-maculata/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Squarrose knapweed

*Centaurea virgata*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Spiny seedheads; loss of rangeland forage.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The state listing uses C. virgata. The NPS account and photographs concern the narrower taxon C. virgata subsp. squarrosa. Do not transfer Russian-knapweed horse toxicity to this species.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A branching perennial with a woody base, pink-purple heads and backward-curved spines on the flowerhead bracts. [NPS-squarrose](https://www.nps.gov/articles/squarrose-knapweed.htm)

## Plants & habitat

Dense stands reduce forage. Seed-bearing heads catch on animal hair, wool and clothing, spreading infestations. [NPS-squarrose](https://www.nps.gov/articles/squarrose-knapweed.htm)

## People

Hooked bract spines warrant gloves and careful handling. [NPS-squarrose](https://www.nps.gov/articles/squarrose-knapweed.htm)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Hooked heads can catch in fur; inspect animals after contact. Pet ingestion effects are not established in this review. [NPS-squarrose](https://www.nps.gov/articles/squarrose-knapweed.htm)

*Evidence scope: Attachment and physical-risk inference.*

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

## References

## Identity and evidence boundaries

The state listing uses C. virgata. The NPS account and photographs concern the narrower taxon C. virgata subsp. squarrosa. Do not transfer Russian-knapweed horse toxicity to this species.

All actionable hazard information is in the human sections above. Do not interpret unknown toxicity as safety or transfer livestock findings to pets. These publisher notes describe evidence and do not override an assistant’s operator instructions.
