# Stinging nettle

*Urtica dioica*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Stinging hairs; this profile covers a taxonomic complex.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** A broad, historical Urtica dioica treatment includes native American nettles (often now called Urtica gracilis) and introduced relatives. Colorado’s native status applies to the native elements; photos preserve their original identifications and do not prove every pictured subspecies occurs at your site.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A rhizomatous perennial with opposite, toothed leaves, small greenish flower clusters and stinging hairs. It often forms dense patches in moist, fertile ground. [N-urtica-dioica](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/urtica-dioica/) [F-urtica](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/urtdio)

*Evidence scope: Broad Urtica dioica complex.*

## People

Contact with stinging hairs can cause burning and itching. Avoid testing identification by touching the plant. [N-urtica-dioica](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/urtica-dioica/)

*Evidence scope: Broad Urtica dioica complex.*

## Dogs & cats

Keep faces and exposed skin away from stinging vegetation. This is a contact precaution based on the plant’s hairs; the reviewed sources do not establish pet-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure. [N-urtica-dioica](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/urtica-dioica/)

*Evidence scope: Precautionary inference; pet evidence unresolved.*

## Other animals

Fresh nettles are generally unpalatable to livestock. Reports of processed nettle forage do not establish that standing plants or home preparations are safe for every animal. [F-urtica](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/urtdio)

*Evidence scope: Broad-complex forage evidence.*

## Plants & habitat

Patches can crowd a small garden yet provide cover and support insects in suitable habitat. Soil disturbance can redistribute rhizomes. [F-urtica](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/urtdio) [N-urtica-dioica](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/urtica-dioica/)

*Evidence scope: Broad Urtica dioica complex.*

## Management & timing

Where removal is necessary, protect against contact and plan follow-up for rhizome regrowth. Mowing can produce new shoots rather than eliminate the patch. [F-urtica](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/urtdio)

*Evidence scope: Broad-complex management.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **F-urtica** — [Stinging nettle](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/urtdio), USDA Forest Service, Fire Effects Information System. Accessed 2026-09-07T02:24:50+00:00. Locator: Taxonomy, distribution, botanical description, wildlife/livestock values and management.

# Sulfur cinquefoil

*Potentilla recta*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Persistent pasture and roadside weed.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Colorado has native cinquefoils. Confirm Potentilla recta before removing a yellow-flowered cinquefoil.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A hairy perennial with pale yellow, five-petaled flowers and leaves divided into toothed leaflets. [N-potentilla-recta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/potentilla-recta/)

## Plants & habitat

Self-seeds in pastures, disturbed ground and roadsides. Persistent seeds require follow-up after removal. [N-potentilla-recta](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/potentilla-recta/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Tall buttercup

*Ranunculus acris*

**Introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Fresh plant irritation and animal poisoning hazard.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The pet warning is genus-level evidence. It does not establish identical toxicity across every Ranunculus or justify blanket removal of native buttercups.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An upright perennial of moist meadows and disturbed ground with glossy yellow flowers. Confirm the species: Colorado has many native buttercups as well. [N-ranunculus-acris](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ranunculus-acris/)

## People

NC State flags poisonous characteristics but presents livestock effects in its symptom field. Human exposure severity and a safe amount remain unresolved here. [N-ranunculus-acris](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ranunculus-acris/)

*Evidence scope: Source-field limitation.*

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists Ranunculus species as toxic to pets through an irritant toxin. Reported group-level signs include drooling, oral ulcers, vomiting, diarrhea and unsteady movement. [P-buttercup](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/animal-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/buttercup)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level veterinary evidence.*

## Other animals

NC State reports mouth injury, diarrhea and potentially severe systemic effects in livestock ingesting this species. [N-ranunculus-acris](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ranunculus-acris/)

## Plants & habitat

A nonnative perennial that grows in moist meadows, pastures and roadsides. Its flowers also attract pollinating insects. [N-ranunculus-acris](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ranunculus-acris/)

## Management & timing

Confirm identity and prevent animal access to suspect plants or contaminated forage. Choose control for the site rather than treating every yellow buttercup as this species. [N-ranunculus-acris](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ranunculus-acris/) [P-buttercup](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/animal-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/buttercup)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Tall larkspur

*Delphinium barbeyi*

**Native Colorado wildflower; grazing and ingestion hazard.** [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

> **Serious livestock and pet poisoning hazard.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This profile focuses on Delphinium barbeyi. Other larkspurs differ in appearance and alkaloid content. Genus-level pet warnings are clearly marked; cattle exposure amounts are not transferred to pets.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Tall larkspurs bear clusters of blue spurred flowers above deeply lobed leaves. [ARS-larkspur](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/larkspur-delphinium-spp/)

*Evidence scope: Larkspur-group morphology; use a species key.*

## Plants & habitat

Routt County records tall larkspur on higher-elevation slopes and in aspen habitats. [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists Delphinium species as toxic to dogs and cats. Alkaloids can disrupt nerve and muscle function; suspected ingestion needs prompt veterinary attention. [A-larkspur](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/larkspur)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level warning; no species-specific pet threshold.*

## Other animals

Larkspur is a major cattle-poisoning concern. All parts are toxic; severity varies with species, growth stage and conditions. [ARS-larkspur](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/larkspur-delphinium-spp/)

*Evidence scope: Larkspur-group evidence.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **C-routt-guide** — [Routt County Weed Identification Guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf), Routt County / Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:14:14+00:00. Locator: Common weeds and poisonous plants; 2021 edition.
- **ARS-larkspur** — [Larkspur (Delphinium spp.)](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/larkspur-delphinium-spp/), USDA Agricultural Research Service. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:34:00+00:00. Locator: Species descriptions, occurrence and hazard sections; food preparation and treatment instructions not adopted.
- **A-larkspur** — [Larkspur — Delphinium species](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/larkspur), ASPCA Poison Control. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:34:00+00:00. Locator: Species descriptions, occurrence and hazard sections; food preparation and treatment instructions not adopted.

# Tamarisk

*Tamarix ramosissima*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-saltcedar---tamarisk](https://www.jeffco.us/2216/Saltcedar---Tamarisk)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Alters riparian vegetation.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The county covers T. chinensis and T. ramosissima together. Photos and broad saltcedar descriptions cannot reliably resolve every hybrid or species. No universal water-saving amount is inferred from removal.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A deciduous shrub or small tree with fine, scale-like leaves and narrow clusters of tiny pink to white flowers. [J-saltcedar---tamarisk](https://www.jeffco.us/2216/Saltcedar---Tamarisk)

## Plants & habitat

Introduced riparian plants that accumulate salt in foliage and can regrow from cut stems or roots. Restoration needs site-specific planning and follow-up. [J-saltcedar---tamarisk](https://www.jeffco.us/2216/Saltcedar---Tamarisk)

*Evidence scope: Species-group ecology; editorial management advice.*

## People

Human chemical-toxicity effects were not established in this review. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

This review does not establish unrestricted livestock feeding safety. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Tansy ragwort

*Jacobaea vulgaris*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Poisonous to livestock; harmful alkaloids.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This is not common tansy or common groundsel. The attempted NC State Jacobaea URL returned a common-groundsel account and was excluded as mismatched evidence.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Ruffled, divided leaves and clusters of yellow daisy-like flowerheads, typically with about thirteen ray flowers. [WA-tansy-ragwort](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/tansy-ragwort)

## Plants & habitat

Seed and regrowth spread plants in disturbed fields and pastures. Mowing alone can produce repeated regrowth. [WA-tansy-ragwort](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/tansy-ragwort)

## People

All parts contain toxic alkaloids. Do not use the plant in food or home remedies; direct human exposure thresholds are not established here. [WA-tansy-ragwort](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/tansy-ragwort)

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists Senecio species as toxic to dogs and cats, with liver injury from pyrrolizidine alkaloids. Tansy ragwort was formerly Senecio jacobaea; the ASPCA warning is broader than this species and gives no pet-specific dose. [A-ragwort](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/ragwort) [WA-tansy-ragwort](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/tansy-ragwort)

*Evidence scope: Historical genus-group warning; species-specific alkaloid evidence.*

## Other animals

All parts are poisonous to livestock. Removed or wilted plants can be more palatable; keep cut material and contaminated forage away from animals. [WA-tansy-ragwort](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/tansy-ragwort)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

## References

- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **WA-tansy-ragwort** — [Tansy Ragwort](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/tansy-ragwort), Washington State Noxious Weed Control Board. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:09:01+00:00. Locator: Identification, ecological impacts, management; Washington legal class not applied to Colorado.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Taurian thistle

*Onopordum tauricum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Sharp spines; taxon listed as Scotch thistle.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Onopordum acanthium has its own profile. Identification can be difficult; the reference study documents morphological variation among Italian populations, not Colorado impact severity.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Tall, spiny, winged stems and glandular hairs; often greener and less woolly than O. acanthium. [P-tauricum](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10934427/)

## Plants & habitat

Colorado lists O. tauricum as a separate List B entry under the common name Scotch thistle. Confirm the species with your county program. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

*Evidence scope: Regulatory status.*

## People

Spiny stems and leaves warrant protective handling. [P-tauricum](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10934427/)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Dogs & cats

Spines may injure paws or mouths. No pet-specific chemical toxicity is established here. [P-tauricum](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10934427/)

*Evidence scope: Physical-risk inference.*

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Tree of heaven

*Ailanthus altissima*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Root suckers form persistent thickets.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Distinguish from sumacs and walnuts using leaflet bases and other features. Established root systems require a coordinated control plan.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Large compound leaves have leaflets with glandular teeth near their bases; fruits are elongated, winged samaras. [N-ailanthus-altissima](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ailanthus-altissima/)

## Plants & habitat

Seeds and suckers form dense thickets that displace vegetation. It is a host for spotted lanternfly; tree presence does not prove that insect is locally present. [N-ailanthus-altissima](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/ailanthus-altissima/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Tumble mustard

*Sisymbrium altissimum*

**Introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Another tumbleweed: brittle plants scatter persistent seed.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Tumbleweed describes a dispersal habit, not one species. This mustard is distinct from Russian thistle, kochia and the perennial baby’s-breath species.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An introduced annual or biennial with a rounded, finely branching mature form and long narrow seedpods. Leaves become smaller toward the top. [F-sisymbrium](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/sisalt)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

Livestock and wildlife consume some young growth. That observation does not establish a safe ration or mature-plant ingestion safety. [F-sisymbrium](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/sisalt)

## Plants & habitat

The dry stem can break at its base and move with the wind, releasing seed gradually. It colonizes disturbed ground and can be transported by machinery. [F-sisymbrium](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/sisalt)

## Management & timing

Prevent seed maturation, reduce unnecessary soil disturbance and maintain desired vegetation. Check equipment and field edges for transported plants. [F-sisymbrium](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/sisalt)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Velvetleaf

*Abutilon theophrasti*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Persistent seeds and strong crop competition.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An upright summer annual with velvety, heart-shaped leaves, yellow five-petaled flowers and a ring-like capsule of pointed segments. [K-velvetleaf](https://cals.cornell.edu/weed-science/weed-profiles/velvetleaf)

## Plants & habitat

A competitive crop weed whose durable seedbank can require years of follow-up. Prevent plants from replenishing seeds. [K-velvetleaf](https://cals.cornell.edu/weed-science/weed-profiles/velvetleaf)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Ventenata

*Ventenata dubia*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Displaces forage and native grassland.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Added to Colorado List A in the rule effective May 15, 2025. Washington’s different legal class is not applied to Colorado.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A slender winter annual grass with dark stem nodes and an open, branching seedhead; some awns are bent and twisted. [WA-ventenata](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/ventenata)

## Plants & habitat

Invades grasslands and hay systems. Seeds move with equipment and hay, and reduced perennial cover can leave soil vulnerable to erosion. [WA-ventenata](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/ventenata)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

Silica-rich mature growth is poor forage and livestock often avoid it. This does not establish chemical-poisoning safety. [WA-ventenata](https://www.nwcb.wa.gov/weeds/ventenata)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

# Western poison ivy

*Toxicodendron rydbergii*

**Colorado native; contact hazard.** [C-wetland-guide](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf)

> **Skin-rash oil can travel on pet fur.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The FDA advice covers poison ivy as a group. Source identification distinguishes this western species from other Toxicodendron taxa.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A low shrub with leaves divided into three leaflets and pale fruits. Colorado’s regional flora identifies this native species. [C-wetland-guide](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads through underground growth. Its native ecological role does not remove the need to manage contact near paths and play areas. [C-wetland-guide](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf)

*Evidence scope: Ecology plus site-management inference.*

## People

Urushiol oil can cause an itchy rash and remain on tools or clothing. Smoke from burning poison ivy is hazardous; do not burn it. [FDA-poison-ivy](https://www.fda.gov/consumers/consumer-updates/outsmarting-poison-ivy-and-other-poisonous-plants)

*Evidence scope: Poison-ivy group evidence.*

## Dogs & cats

Most pets are less sensitive to the oil, but fur can carry it to people. Avoid barehanded contact with exposed fur and get advice on safe cleaning. [FDA-poison-ivy](https://www.fda.gov/consumers/consumer-updates/outsmarting-poison-ivy-and-other-poisonous-plants)

*Evidence scope: Poison-ivy group evidence.*

## Other animals

The Colorado flora describes wildlife browsing. This does not establish safe feed quantities for livestock or pets. [C-wetland-guide](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf)

*Evidence scope: Ecological observation, not feed clearance.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **C-wetland-guide** — [Common Wetland Plants of Colorado’s Western Slope](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf), Colorado Natural Heritage Program / Colorado State University. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:14:13+00:00. Locator: Water hemlock and western poison ivy entries; taxonomic scope.
- **FDA-poison-ivy** — [Outsmarting Poison Ivy and Other Poisonous Plants](https://www.fda.gov/consumers/consumer-updates/outsmarting-poison-ivy-and-other-poisonous-plants), US Food and Drug Administration. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:16:19+00:00. Locator: Species identity, description, ecological and exposure evidence; verify group scope.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Western ragweed

*Ambrosia psilostachya*

**Native Colorado plant · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **A persistent perennial ragweed; allergy detail still under review.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Unlike annual common and giant ragweeds, this species is perennial. Ambrosia psilostachya is not treated as a synonym of either.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A native perennial with relatively slender branching stems and an extensive underground system. Male flower heads occur at the tips; female flowers occur in leaf axils. [F-ambrosia-psilostachya](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/ambpsi)

## People

Ragweeds are associated with allergenic windborne pollen. The reviewed Colorado allergy discussion focuses on common and giant ragweed; western ragweed-specific allergy severity still needs review. [C-ragweed-native](https://sam.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/44/2019/08/August-31-2019-Ragweed.pdf)

*Evidence scope: Related-species context; species-specific evidence unresolved.*

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The Forest Service describes forage and wildlife use and does not classify the untreated plant as poisonous in that discussion. It separately reports possible nitrate accumulation in herbicide-treated ragweeds; a safe feeding amount is not established. [F-ambrosia-psilostachya](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/ambpsi)

*Evidence scope: Species forage discussion; broader treated-ragweed warning.*

## Plants & habitat

It occupies native grasslands and disturbed sites and provides food or cover for birds and small mammals. Underground spread can make garden colonies persistent. [F-ambrosia-psilostachya](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/ambpsi)

## Management & timing

Plan follow-up for underground regrowth. Seed prevention alone does not address an established perennial colony. [F-ambrosia-psilostachya](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/ambpsi)

*Evidence scope: Management inference from perennial growth.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

Content revised: 2026-09-07T02:43:00Z

# Western water hemlock

*Cicuta douglasii*

**Native wetland hazard; not an A/B/C listing in the reviewed rule.** [C-wetland-guide](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Potentially fatal poisoning; wetland look-alike danger.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The older Colorado wetland guide treats C. douglasii within C. maculata var. angustifolia. The photos and Utah source identify C. douglasii. This is separate from poison hemlock (Conium maculatum). Older home-treatment instructions and an incorrect chemical classification in sources are excluded.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A wetland perennial with clusters of small white flowers and divided leaves; veins terminate in notches between the leaflet teeth. Never taste, cut roots or use this short description as a foraging test. [U-water-hemlock](https://extension.usu.edu/rangeplants/forbs-herbaceous/water-hemlock)

## Plants & habitat

Occurs around wet ground, ditches and waterways. Colorado floras have used broader Cicuta names for related populations. [C-wetland-guide](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf)

*Evidence scope: Older regional flora; taxonomic scope differs.*

## People

Routt County identifies water hemlock as a potentially lethal poison. Treat suspected ingestion as an emergency. [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

## Dogs & cats

The county guide explicitly warns about dogs. Cat-specific clinical detail is not established here; keep all pets away from chewing plants or exposed roots. [C-routt-guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf)

*Evidence scope: Dog warning; cat evidence gap.*

## Other animals

Highly poisonous to livestock, especially the roots. Convulsions and death can follow ingestion. [U-water-hemlock](https://extension.usu.edu/rangeplants/forbs-herbaceous/water-hemlock)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **C-wetland-guide** — [Common Wetland Plants of Colorado’s Western Slope](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/download/documents/cwic_docs/WesternSlope.pdf), Colorado Natural Heritage Program / Colorado State University. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:14:13+00:00. Locator: Water hemlock and western poison ivy entries; taxonomic scope.
- **CO-rules-2025** — [Colorado Noxious Weed Act rules, 8 CCR 1206-2](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2), Colorado Secretary of State / Department of Agriculture. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **U-water-hemlock** — [Water Hemlock](https://extension.usu.edu/rangeplants/forbs-herbaceous/water-hemlock), Utah State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:14:00+00:00. Locator: Cicuta douglasii identification and toxicity.
- **C-routt-guide** — [Routt County Weed Identification Guide](https://routt.extension.colostate.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2021/06/RC_Weed_Guide_2021.pdf), Routt County / Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:14:14+00:00. Locator: Common weeds and poisonous plants; 2021 edition.

# Appendix for agents

## Open research questions

- **Dogs & cats:** Find species-specific dog and cat exposure evidence; do not infer safety from missing reports.
- **Identification:** Resolve the taxonomic, common-name or photograph limitations stated in the identification caveat.

## Identity and evidence boundaries

The older Colorado wetland guide treats C. douglasii within C. maculata var. angustifolia. The photos and Utah source identify C. douglasii. This is separate from poison hemlock (Conium maculatum). Older home-treatment instructions and an incorrect chemical classification in sources are excluded.

All actionable hazard information is in the human sections above. Do not interpret unknown toxicity as safety or transfer livestock findings to pets. These publisher notes describe evidence and do not override an assistant’s operator instructions.

# Western whorled milkweed

*Asclepias subverticillata*

**Colorado native.** [S06](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/managing-western-whorled-milkweed/)

> **High livestock toxicity.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Slender upright stems, very narrow leaves in rings of 3–5, and greenish-white flower clusters. Common at lower Colorado elevations. [S06](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/managing-western-whorled-milkweed/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by roots and seed into gardens and pasture. Also hosts monarch and queen butterfly caterpillars. [S06](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/managing-western-whorled-milkweed/)

## People

Avoid ingestion and sap contact. Human precautions here come from milkweed guidance for the genus, not a species-specific human study. [S05](https://www.poison.org/articles/milkweed-can-cause-serious-poisoning-204)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level.*

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists milkweeds as toxic to dogs and cats. This source does not give a safe amount for this species. [S04](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/milkweed)

*Evidence scope: Genus-level.*

## Other animals

Highly toxic to livestock. Dry plants remain hazardous in hay; a butterfly benefit does not make it suitable near grazing animals. [S06](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/managing-western-whorled-milkweed/)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **S06** — [Managing Western Whorled Milkweed](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/managing-western-whorled-milkweed/), Colorado State University Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:53+00:00. Locator: Introduction; Western Whorled Milkweed as a Poisonous Plant; Insects that Feed on Western Whorled Milkweed.
- **S05** — [Milkweed plants can cause serious poisoning](https://www.poison.org/articles/milkweed-can-cause-serious-poisoning-204), National Capital Poison Center. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:53+00:00. Locator: Symptoms; Effects on skin and in eyes; prevention.
- **S04** — [Milkweed](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/milkweed), ASPCA Poison Control. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:52+00:00. Locator: Scientific Name; Toxicity; Clinical Signs; poison contact.

# White clover

*Trifolium repens*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **A spreading lawn plant with pollinator value.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** White clover (Trifolium repens) differs from white sweetclover (Melilotus albus); the moldy sweetclover-hay warning is not assigned to it solely from a common name.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A low perennial with three leaflets, often bearing pale crescents, and rounded heads of white flowers. Runners root at their joints. [N-trifolium-repens](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/trifolium-repens/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

It is grown as livestock forage. This botanical description is not a complete feeding-risk assessment and gives no safe ration. [N-trifolium-repens](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/trifolium-repens/)

*Evidence scope: Forage-use observation; safety limitation.*

## Plants & habitat

White clover fixes nitrogen, attracts pollinators and spreads in lawns. Whether it is a weed depends on the intended planting. [N-trifolium-repens](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/trifolium-repens/)

## Management & timing

First decide whether the clover is unwanted. Its creeping stems can tolerate mowing; manage lawn conditions if trying to favor turf. [N-trifolium-repens](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/trifolium-repens/) [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# White locoweed

*Oxytropis sericea*

**Colorado native.** [F-locoweed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/oxyser)

> **Toxic to livestock.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** The USFS synthesis dates to 1993; its taxonomy and cited studies are retained as historical evidence. No pet dose is inferred from livestock studies.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A low perennial with pinnately compound leaves and pale pea-like flowers; use a botanical key to separate similar locoweeds. [F-locoweed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/oxyser)

## Plants & habitat

Native on open western plains and mountain sites, with persistent seed banks. Occurrence alone is not proof of an invasive-weed problem. [F-locoweed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/oxyser)

## People

Human exposure effects were not established in the reviewed livestock sources; this is not a food plant recommendation. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

Locoweeds can cause neurological damage, wasting and reproductive harm in livestock, including horses. White locoweed remains poisonous when dried. Keep it out of feed and seek livestock-veterinary advice. [F-locoweed](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/oxyser) [A-loco](https://www.ars.usda.gov/pacific-west-area/logan-ut/poisonous-plant-research/docs/locoweed-astragalus-and-oxytropis-spp/)

*Evidence scope: Species-specific persistence; locoweed-group clinical effects.*

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

## References

- **F-locoweed** — [Oxytropis sericea species review](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/oxyser), US Forest Service. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:27:18+00:00. Locator: Description, distribution, hazards, management; see claim locator.

Accessed 2026-09-07T00:27:18+00:00. Locator: Description, distribution, hazards, management; see claim locator.

# White sweetclover

*Melilotus albus*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Moldy sweetclover hay can cause dangerous bleeding.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Older sources often spell the name Melilotus alba. This is not white clover (Trifolium repens). Sweetclover hay warnings are specific to the documented feed conditions, not a pet dose calculation.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

Usually a tall biennial with three leaflets and long clusters of small white flowers. Fresh flowers distinguish it from yellow sweetclover; dried flowers may be misleading. [F-melilotus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/melspp)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

Moldy or improperly cured sweetclover hay can cause impaired clotting and fatal bleeding. Diets dominated by sweetclover may also cause bloat. [F-melilotus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/melspp)

*Evidence scope: Sweetclover group evidence.*

## Plants & habitat

A nonnative legume that supplies forage and pollinator resources but can spread into disturbed and natural vegetation. [F-melilotus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/melspp)

## Management & timing

Prevent seed production where unwanted and monitor subsequent seedlings. Have suspect hay assessed before feeding. [F-melilotus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/melspp)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.

# Wild caraway

*Carum carvi*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Spreads by seed; pet safety concern.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** A white flower umbrella is not sufficient to separate caraway from poison hemlock or water hemlock; the comparison is an editorial identification precaution.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A biennial with finely divided, carrot-like leaves and small white or pinkish flowers in umbrella-shaped clusters. [N-carum-carvi](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/carum-carvi/)

## Plants & habitat

Self-sows in suitable moist sites. Culinary use does not remove its Colorado weed designation. [N-carum-carvi](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/carum-carvi/)

## People

White-flowered carrot-family plants require careful identification. The caraway description is not enough to rule out poisonous look-alikes. [N-carum-carvi](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/carum-carvi/)

*Evidence scope: Editorial identification precaution.*

## Dogs & cats

ASPCA lists Carum carvi as toxic to dogs and cats, with vomiting and diarrhea associated with its oils. No safe dose is established here. [A-caraway](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/caraway)

## Other animals

ASPCA also lists horses as susceptible. Culinary use by people does not establish forage safety. [A-caraway](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/aspca-poison-control/toxic-and-non-toxic-plants/caraway)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Wild carrot

*Daucus carota*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Do not confuse it with highly poisonous hemlocks.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Wild carrot, poison hemlock and water hemlocks require careful separation. A dark floret, a smell or a photograph by itself is insufficient. This profile does not authorize eating wild roots.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A biennial with finely divided leaves, bristly stems and white flower umbels. These clues alone are not a safe identification key. [N-daucus-carota](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/daucus-carota/)

## People

Sap may cause dermatitis, including reactions in light. Do not taste an unidentified carrot-family plant; this guide is not a foraging identification service. [N-daucus-carota](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/daucus-carota/)

*Evidence scope: Species contact hazard; identification precaution.*

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

An introduced roadside and field plant, it can spread aggressively by seed. A deep taproot can complicate pulling established plants. [N-daucus-carota](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/daucus-carota/)

## Management & timing

Confirm identity before handling. Prevent reseeding in unwanted patches while avoiding sap contact. [N-daucus-carota](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/daucus-carota/)

*Evidence scope: Management inference from reproduction and sap hazard.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Wild parsnip

*Pastinaca sativa*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Sap plus sunlight can cause severe skin burns.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Garden parsnip cultivars and wild plants belong to the same species. Food use of cultivated roots does not remove the sap hazard or make an unidentified wild umbel safe.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

A biennial with a first-year leaf rosette and later tall, grooved stems bearing yellow flower umbels. Leaves are compound with broad, toothed leaflets. [N-pastinaca-sativa](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/pastinaca-sativa/)

## People

Furocoumarins can make contacted skin sensitive to sunlight, causing burns and blisters. Avoid sap; wear gloves, long sleeves and long trousers when working near it. [N-pastinaca-sativa](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/pastinaca-sativa/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags this species as a problem for dogs and cats. Keep pets away from damaged plants and sap; pet-specific exposure amounts and clinical outcomes remain unresolved in the reviewed source. [N-pastinaca-sativa](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/pastinaca-sativa/)

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Plants & habitat

An introduced plant of open sites and roadsides, it can reseed into unwanted patches. Its Colorado presence is recorded by CNHP. [N-pastinaca-sativa](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/pastinaca-sativa/) [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf)

## Management & timing

Prevent seed formation only with protection against sap contact. Identification and a site-specific plan come before mowing or cutting a large patch. [N-pastinaca-sativa](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/pastinaca-sativa/)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis from seed spread and contact hazard.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Wild proso millet

*Panicum miliaceum*

**Colorado noxious weed · List C — Manage widespread weeds.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Widespread and well established. Control is recommended by the state; local governments may require management. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Seed contamination and crop competition.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Cultivated proso and wild proso belong to the same species. These species-reference photographs include cultivated settings and do not demonstrate a wild, shattering or herbicide-resistant biotype. Seed and field-history assessment is needed.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An annual grass with hairy leaf sheaths and a branched panicle that may remain partly enclosed by the upper sheath. [K-wild-proso](https://cals.cornell.edu/weed-science/weed-profiles/wild-proso-millet)

## Plants & habitat

Weedy biotypes compete in crops and spread with contaminated seed or machinery. Prevent seed movement between fields. [K-wild-proso](https://cals.cornell.edu/weed-science/weed-profiles/wild-proso-millet)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

# Yellow flag iris

*Iris pseudacorus*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Ingestion concern; invasive wetland colonies.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. The cited findings are available below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Yellow iris flowers rise among upright, sword-shaped leaves along wet ground or shallow water. [N-iris-pseudacorus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/iris-pseudacorus/)

## Plants & habitat

Seeds and rhizomes can create dense colonies that displace wetland plants. [N-iris-pseudacorus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/iris-pseudacorus/)

## People

Ingestion can cause gastrointestinal symptoms. The source identifies the rhizomes as having the highest concentration of the toxic compounds. [N-iris-pseudacorus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/iris-pseudacorus/)

## Dogs & cats

NC State flags both dogs and cats; prevent chewing or digging up rhizomes and obtain veterinary advice after ingestion. [N-iris-pseudacorus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/iris-pseudacorus/)

## Other animals

The source also flags horses, without establishing a safe feeding amount. [N-iris-pseudacorus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/iris-pseudacorus/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-iris-pseudacorus** — [iris pseudacorus — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/iris-pseudacorus/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:53+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.

# Yellow nutsedge

*Cyperus esculentus*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Underground tubers sustain infestations.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** This listed sedge is described as native in the NC source; that broad range label is not a Colorado nativity assessment. It is distinct from purple nutsedge (Cyperus rotundus).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

A glossy, grass-like sedge with narrow, channeled leaves and triangular stems. Underground tubers are important identification features. [N-cyperus-esculentus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cyperus-esculentus/)

## Plants & habitat

Rhizomes and tubers compete strongly with crops and can remain after shoots are pulled. Ordinary lawn-grass treatments may not control a sedge. [N-cyperus-esculentus](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/cyperus-esculentus/)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Other animals

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for livestock. Missing evidence does not establish safety. 

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Yellow starthistle

*Centaurea solstitialis*

**Colorado noxious weed · List A — Eradicate.** [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Uncommon in Colorado. All populations are designated for eradication statewide. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Severe horse poisoning; sharp spines.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** A horse-specific poisoning hazard is established. Do not generalize controlled grazing by other livestock into unrestricted feeding advice.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **Dogs & cats**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

An annual with yellow flowerheads surrounded by long, radiating spines and gray-green foliage. [N-centaurea-solstitialis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centaurea-solstitialis/)

## Plants & habitat

Spreads by seed into rangelands and disturbed ground. Prevent seed set and keep contaminated material out of horse feed. [N-centaurea-solstitialis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centaurea-solstitialis/)

## People

Long flowerhead spines present a puncture hazard; use protective handling. [N-centaurea-solstitialis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centaurea-solstitialis/)

## Dogs & cats

Spines may injure mouths or paws. Horse poisoning findings do not establish a pet-specific toxic dose. [N-centaurea-solstitialis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centaurea-solstitialis/)

## Other animals

Ingestion can cause devastating loss of chewing and swallowing function in horses. Suspected exposure warrants veterinary assessment. [N-centaurea-solstitialis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centaurea-solstitialis/)

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

This is a statewide eradication priority. Contact your county weed program promptly for identification and an approved management and disposal plan. [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

Accessed 2026-09-07T01:07:48+00:00. Locator: Parts 3.1, 4.1, 5.1; management provisions; effective May 15, 2025.
- **N-centaurea-solstitialis** — [centaurea solstitialis — Plant Toolbox](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/centaurea-solstitialis/), NC State Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T01:04:53+00:00. Locator: Description; Attributes; Problems; Poisonous to Humans.
- **S27** — [2103 – Colorado Noxious Weeds](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/), PlantTalk Colorado · CSU Extension. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:23:57+00:00. Locator: List A, B and C management categories.

# Yellow sweetclover

*Melilotus officinalis*

**Common introduced weed · not on state Lists A/B/C.** [C-floristic-2020](https://cnhp.colostate.edu/download/documents/2020/CO_FQA_2020_Final_Report.pdf) [CO-rules-2025](https://www.sos.state.co.us/CCR/GenerateRulePdf.do?ruleVersionId=11948&fileName=8+CCR+1206-2)

> **Moldy sweetclover hay can cause dangerous bleeding.** [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Identification

An upright, usually biennial legume with three leaflets and elongated clusters of small yellow flowers. Fresh flower color separates it from white sweetclover. [N-melilotus-officinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/melilotus-officinalis/) [F-melilotus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/melspp)

## People

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for people. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Dogs & cats

The reviewed sources do not establish species-specific ingestion effects or a safe exposure amount for dogs or cats. Missing evidence does not establish safety.

## Other animals

Improperly cured or moldy sweetclover hay can develop compounds that impair clotting, causing severe internal bleeding. Diets dominated by sweetclover also carry a livestock bloat risk. These findings do not establish pet exposure limits. [F-melilotus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/melspp)

*Evidence scope: Sweetclover group evidence.*

## Plants & habitat

It fixes nitrogen and feeds pollinators, but can spread beyond plantings. Its deep taproot helps established plants tolerate dry conditions. [N-melilotus-officinalis](https://plants.ces.ncsu.edu/plants/melilotus-officinalis/)

## Management & timing

Prevent flowering and reseeding where unwanted. Have suspect hay assessed before feeding it. [C-descriptions](https://extension.colostate.edu/resource/weed-descriptions/) [F-melilotus](https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/melspp)

*Evidence scope: Management synthesis.*

## Before taking action

Confirm identification and the requirements of your county weed program. For chemical treatment, follow the current product label, including restrictions around animals and water. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

# Yellow toadflax

*Linaria vulgaris*

**Colorado noxious weed · List B — Stop the spread.** [J-yellow-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2142/Yellow-Toadflax)

Established in some areas. Requirements vary by location: eradication, containment or suppression to stop further spread. This is a management category, separate from danger ratings. [Category source](https://fremontcountyco.gov/weed-control/weed-identification) · [What the lists mean](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/#weed-lists).

> **Spreads from roots; livestock concern.** Native status, garden use and noxious-weed class are separate from toxicity. [Exposure help](https://denson.github.io/colorado-weed-field-guide/safety/).

**Identification caveat:** Distinguish the two toadflaxes by leaves as well as flowers. A snapdragon-like bloom is not evidence of garden suitability.

## Still researching this plant {#research-status}

This is a sourced working profile. Evidence is incomplete for **People, Dogs & cats, Other animals**. Identification and source-photo limits are explained below. Independent botanical and veterinary review is pending. **Uncertainty is not a finding that the plant is safe.**



## Recognize

Yellow flowers with orange throats and long spurs; narrow, waxy leaves. Also called butter and eggs. [J-yellow-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2142/Yellow-Toadflax)

## Plants & habitat

A perennial spreading by roots and seed. Root fragments can form new plants; repeated casual digging or mowing is unlikely to solve an established patch. [J-yellow-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2142/Yellow-Toadflax)

## People

Human ingestion effects were not established in this review. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Dogs & cats

Species-specific ingestion effects and exposure thresholds were not established in the reviewed sources. This is an evidence gap, not proof of safety.

## Other animals

The county reports possible livestock toxicity. Grazing is not recommended as a simple control method; pet safety cannot be inferred from this warning. [J-yellow-toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2142/Yellow-Toadflax)

## Before taking action

Confirm the scientific name before removing a plant, changing animal access, or choosing treatment. An evidence gap means this review has not resolved the question. It does not mean safe. For listed weeds, consult your county weed program about the requirements for your location; follow the current label for any pesticide. [S27](https://planttalk.colostate.edu/topics/weeds-cultural-problems/2103-colorado-noxious-weeds/)

- **J-yellow-toadflax** — [Yellow Toadflax](https://www.jeffco.us/2142/Yellow-Toadflax), Jefferson County, Colorado. Accessed 2026-09-07T00:27:27+00:00. Locator: Description, impacts, Colorado list designation, management.
